- Kest's six-clause instrument of the third named **the eighth of this month, at the top of the tide, being a spring tide**, held open at his pleasure. That date is now the day the plan was supposed to exist and does not.

### The batch's money chain, which later chapters must not break

- Start on the seventh: **five marks in the bag, nothing in the tin**, air pot a third gone, note still written up in the chandler's own book as **eleven weeks into six** with nothing paid on it since the twenty-fifth of last month.
- **12th, Saturday:** three marks handed to the circuit office for the entering of the plan and the fee of the countersign, written into the clerk's own small book; **two of the five are set against the chandler's note** and are to go on it on the Sunday night; the fee of three comes back the same afternoon on the refusal. The bag is therefore **three in hand and two spoken for**, which is five.
- **13th, Sunday:** Elias Voke pays **fourteen** for the mole's second seam over two days, and the whole fourteen is disposed of on the page: **four to the air, two to rope and the day's food, two to the harbor's share, four to the note**, and the two set against the note on Saturday go on it the same night, which is **six on the note and the first money on it since the twenty-fifth of last month**. Two of the fourteen are unspent and stay in the bag. Bag **five**, and the breakdown the book carries is two of it air for the next tide, two of it Voke's money, one of it the leftover of a Saturday in the middle of October. **Nothing has left the bag on the fourteenth**, so the five in Chapter 40's closing image are the same five.
- **Total air bought in this batch: four marks. Since the twenty-eighth of last month: thirteen. Across the yard's life: twenty-seven.** The pot is a third gone at both ends and thirteen marks have not put a mark back into it.
- **The chandler's note has had six marks on it in three days** (two set against it on the twelfth and all six paid on the night of the thirteenth), which is the first money on it since the twenty-fifth of last month and **twelve marks lighter than it was on the fifteenth of last month** — six across Batch 0002 and six here. **The chandler has not written a new figure and neither has Calder.** The note is still *eleven weeks into six* on the chandler's own books, and that is the coldest line in the batch.
- **The pump, the clamp stock, the cordage and the bench chest come out of the clerk's tin on the fourteenth at half past nine**, the gear still assessed to eighteen and the tin still empty, and the pump is still cracked in its housing with no replacement bought and none to be had before the spring.
- **The twenty-four marks of shoal iron stay on the stack.** A third of the sale is entered to Sella Brigg and **she has declined to press it in a room with a naval officer in it and has asked to be paid in February.**
- **Bevin Rook is owed forty marks: sixteen for the reading of the book from the fourteenth of October at four a week for four weeks, and twenty-four for the hand from the twenty-ninth at four a week for six weeks.** Both claims stand. Neither is reduced. There is no mark behind either.
- The first balance sheet of the batch is the yard's tenth-day one from the fourth of last month, unchanged. What has changed on it: the defense is discharged, the bottom is gone, the harbor eighth and the notice stand, and there is now a disputed claim of nineteen winters against the harbor authority that is somebody else's debt and somebody else's cost.

### Documents and instruments created in this batch

1. **The hearing record of the sitting of the seventh and of the resumed sitting of the fourteenth**, in the harbor authority's own book, with the clerk's pen, with the *Vigil's* claim and the yard's three-word objection on one page, with four grounds of discharge, with the reason of two hundred and forty words, with a fourth *not stated*, with a twenty-four-mark and sixteen-mark wage claim, with a mother saying *it is the original*, and with Elias Voke's disputed claim for nineteen winters of moorage dues.
2. **The slip on the anchor-bearing sheet** in the clerk's drawer, reading *not used at the sitting of the seventh, at the yard's request, by the reeve's ruling*, signed by both men.
3. **Kest's entry of the tenth of November**, longer than any in nine years, carrying the refusal to strike the fifth-seam sighting, the chart claim, the municipal cost, and the date, all four in one hand.
4. **The circuit office's request of the eleventh**, four paragraphs, under the seal line *Magister Ilya Serr, senior Registry examiner*, asking for two certifications: of the ground description in the hearing record with the market-wall sheets, and of the public provenance duplicate as a true record of its objects and their origin.
5. **Mara Venn's written refusal of the eleventh**, four grounds, entered over their heads as *the grounds of the refusal*, with the line *lodged for countersign* and the date **the twenty-second of this month**, which is not hers to choose.
6. **The six-clause plan and its reason**, lodged at the circuit office on the twelfth, stamped, and **returned uncountersigned the same afternoon with four grounds**, the first of which uses the word *negotiable*.
7. **The plan's reason**, two hundred and forty words, in the crew's book and read out in the hearing room: two descriptions of a bolted rim, four words in each, the two close and unmeasured, the comparison asked of the office and not the yard, the purpose unknown, and the yard not asking for anything in the custody room.
8. **The withdrawal of ten yards**, written on a fresh market-wall sheet in Calder's own hand: *the difference is withdrawn by the yard. See the sheet inside, which says why* — and the sheet inside, with the three points, the limits, and the tender's count qualified and the diver's own count given.
9. **The record of the copy consent**: the ten crew-only copies shown on a hearing-room table, one at a time, in daylight, with the clerk, and not carried off it; counted as two on the market wall, three on other crews' walls and five rolled in oilcloth in the bench chest, by the officer's man, with the hour.
10. **The yard's written purpose for the sitting of the twenty-eighth**, headed only, on a blank slate, due with the harbor authority **on the twenty-first** and to be read into the record with the door shut **on the twenty-second**. **Not written. The slate is on the bench.**
11. **The notice of the twenty-eighth of November** — the reeve's own notice of a sitting on a one-season provisional working charter, to the yard, to the Morrow circuit office and to the *Vigil*, with the purpose due on the twenty-first and the date fixed. **The charter itself is not granted in this batch and must not be.**

### The three points, as they now stand

- **The difference of ten yards is withdrawn by the yard, and what is in no record and on no wall is the difference stated as a measurement.** This is the one version: the two figures were of different kinds — a tender's count from a boat with a hand lead on a moving bottom, and the harbor authority's own run along its own course with a wire on a lead, corrected by a column nobody had read — and a difference equal to the tolerance of one of the two numbers is an agreement and not a measurement, which is the *Vigil's* officer's word in the record of the eighth. **What is on a wall is the withdrawal**: a fresh market-wall sheet in Calder's own hand, *the difference is withdrawn by the yard. See the sheet inside, which says why*, nailed under the Navy's paper where a man has to lift a corner, and the yard's three sheets carry three points with the difference taken off them. **What is on no record and on no wall is the interval asserted as a figure.** *(The Batch 0005 review found this lock stated three incompatible ways across this file and `state/open-threads.md`. The sentence above is the single version and it is what Chapter 41's purpose now says.)*
- **The first point is not a point.** It is a bedded seam of transparent material in the mole's own foundation stone, running north under the rubble to about two boat lengths, deeper at the north end, three keys seen, and the yard has been using its **south end** as a position because the boat lay there. The tolerance of the first figure is **not better than the length of the seam, being about two boat lengths, being about forty feet.**
- **The diver's own count is entered at last**: Bevin Rook, on his own statement, gave **seven and eleven** against the tender's **nine and ten and a half** on the twenty-eighth of October, and did not enter it then because it could not be checked. It is in the record on the fourteenth, and it is the only check on the first point there is.
- **What is left standing:** three places on one bearing where the bottom comes up, and nowhere else on two miles of a course the harbor authority itself sounded; and the office's own figure of **one in thirty-six on a firm-clay flat** at fifteen fathoms and two corrected, at about two miles and a quarter from the mole. **What is not:** what is under the water, whether the three things are one thing, and any purpose.
- The **no-line ruling stands and was not lifted.** The **word *placed*** survives only inside the hearing record of the purpose of the twenty-seventh, and the yard's own copy of the purpose still has it struck out.

### The same shoal, and what the join actually is

The Movement 4 requirement — placing the *Vigil's* claim and the seized material on the same ground by physical measurement and the provenance duplicate and not by the echo — is met in Chapter 33 and is met **against** the yard's own filed purpose, which is withdrawn in public:

- **The plate** was lifted off a transparent keel on the shoal's inner edge at eight fathoms on the twelfth of October, on a permit the harbor authority issued, inside the harbor line, and **the ground the permit named is ground the *Vigil's* claim as filed describes**. The claim was lodged on the fourteenth at ten-forty, and the two days between the permit and the claim are the only two days nobody in the file has been able to do anything with.
- **The shard** came up off the mole's toe at the third seam in about eight feet, and **the mole's toe is not the ground the claim describes**, and Calder says so in a hearing room before anybody asks, and the yard's attempt to put it there was the purpose of the twenty-seventh and the bearing sheet, and both are now withdrawn.
- **The join that does the work is ordinary iron and a rope**: the harbor authority's own board tag on its own ground tackle, the reeve's rag float with the chalk seam number, the authority's eight-year-old unclaimed-wreck list naming the *Alder Reach* with her ground tackle gone in a westerly, the fouled ground line's right-hand lay and left-handed dressing from east of the marsh, and **a bright saw kerf in the crown shackle of a working anchor that was loaded again.** Checkable by any man with a rule, a lead and a boat, and it accuses nobody.
- **The fouled line itself is in Registry custody** under one of the two numbers and was not produced; the yard worked from the provenance duplicate, which is a record of the object and not a copy of anything inside it.
- **No custody object returns and no supervised look is granted.** The plate drawing was produced because it is the crew's own drawing; the two objects were not asked for and were not offered.

### The fifth-seam plate, and why it is not reached

- Still in eleven feet in the mole's own foundation stone at the fifth seam, still seen and not measured and not touched, still entered in the reeve's own book with three named witnesses.
- **The *Vigil* moved to strike the sighting on the ground that an officer's observation made in his own breach is not the office's observation. Kest refused, on the record, and paid for it.** He must keep the notice of the thirty-first standing whole and unnarrowed to keep the sighting valid, which keeps the harbor authority's own moorings, fenders and berths out of service with no end date, and **nineteen winters of herring dues are now a disputed claim against the harbor authority in a hearing record.**
- **The plan for one supervised look was written, lodged, and refused on four correct grounds on the twelfth of November.** The grounds: the office will not countersign an instrument putting a person inside the closure its own notice created; a countersignature on application makes the notice a first refusal; the office will not compare a thing in its custody with a thing in a wall on a plan produced by a crew the notice bars; and the office declines both the plan and the conservator's certificate without treating either as a comment on a person.
- **Kest refused to ask for the countersign** and said so in a hearing room on the seventh, and the reason he gave is that a reeve's request is a pressure and a pressure may be refused without a reason. Calder asked anyway, on his own paper, and was refused. **The panel stays foregone. It is in March.**

### Bodies and the crew

- **Bevin Rook, 22.** Left hand a fortnight along, the fourth and third, cannot close, wood across the heel of the palm, **left arm in the sling, right hand writes**. The bonesetter says *hold, and leave it*; Alden Marr re-bound it once more and will not do it again. **He goes back in the water on the tenth of December**, under a tender who is not him, and the date was entered in a hearing room. **He is owed forty marks and the yard has five.** He is the reader of the crew's book, which is **a trade and not a wage until the tenth of December** and stops on that date whether or not there is work.
- **Calder Marr.** Off the bottom again from the fifteenth of November by the reeve's own undertaking. Tinnitus with a third-of-a-second shadow, forearm ungloved, hearing compensated by four people. **He has told Mara Venn what the mark is and has entered it in nothing.**
- **Nessa Pike.** Tender's cleat, pump, fall, board, slate. She has now overruled Calder four times since the twenty-eighth of October, on water, on the record, on the *placed* word, and on the interval and the reason, and has been right every time. She wrote the plan's reason's method and refused to write the sentence about the ferry.
- **Tobias Wren, 61.** Said the half of April out loud on a Sunday at nine o'clock so that two people in the yard had said it. Two feet of oak baulk out of the closed face on the third, in the book, for the reeve to have. The *Kittiwake* is not being moved to the slip.
- **Elias Voke**, herring skipper of the *Anna Ray*, nineteen winters of moorage dues, fifty-one. He is not an antagonist and he is not a friend; he is a man with a berth, and the chapter's ordinary person with a number in it.
- **Bevin Rook's mother** came to the hearing at half past ten with the surgeon's original in her apron, was told in a room, out loud, by a man in a coat, and said *it is the original*, and went four steps back.

### The delayed note

- **Heard seven times and logged five across the two batches.** The logged occasions are the twenty-sixth, the second and the fourth of last month, and in this batch **the eighth**, at four-forty-six in the road, and **the fourteenth**, an hour after noon.
- **The eighth of November is the first time a naval record has it.** The officer of the *Vigil* said aloud in the road that he did not know what it was and neither did Calder, and the officer's man wrote the hour and the state of the water on his own slate, and Bevin Rook wrote *two records, same hour, no source* under his own entry and asked the rating to write the same.
- **The twelfth of November is the one occasion in this batch on which nothing was written**, at the top of the make on the mole steps, because Nessa Pike's hand was on Calder's shoulder and the rule in the yard is that a man who hears a knock in a bottom does not get to see the pencil first.
- **It is not resolved and Bevin Rook's book is never going to resolve it.** The hundred and forty entries in that book now include four that say *knock, delayed, no source, not written as a voice*, and Mara Venn's argument in Chapter 38 is that those four lines, read beside a naval file that has the same sound in it with a time on it, are the record the hiding has made.

### Costs the volume may not waive again

- **Money:** four marks of air, two marks for the plan's entering fee, fourteen earned on the second seam and twelve of it spent the same night, six marks on the chandler's note in three days, forty marks owed to a boy of twenty-two, and nineteen winters of herring dues now a disputed claim against the harbor authority.
- **Body:** a crushed hand and six weeks of a boy's wages, twenty-four marks the yard has not got, and a boy who goes back in the water on a date fixed in a hearing room.
- **Standing:** the defense is **discharged and not renewed**; the bottom is gone; the yard's geography was withdrawn in public; the market wall is under a naval paper; clause three stands and cannot be lifted in this harbor; and the crew has no boat, no permit and no means of work on the authority's ground.
- **Time:** eight days, two hearings, a plan lodged and refused in one afternoon, a job done in two days, and a purpose not written.
- **Authority:** Calder has given away the only thing the yard owned, which is the exclusive use of ten pieces of paper, and has put ten yards off the wall himself, and the plate at the fifth seam is still in the wall.

## Proposed canon changes awaiting a planning phase — CLOSED, all items ruled

**This list is closed. Every item that stood here was ruled by the Volume 01 volume audit on 26 September 2026 and the rulings are recorded under *Rulings of the Volume 01 volume audit* at the foot of this file, in `state/open-threads.md`, and in `reviews/volume-01/volume-01-close.md` Part 2.**

- Items 1–5 (the first entry's hand cost; the Oren mark in Chapter 38; the bellwright's core and the craft of it; the *Aster Gannet* under the mole; the midpoint's three-step finding) — **all ruled or ratified.** None remains a proposed canon change.
- Item 6 (the hearing room's slate wall) — **confirmed as an institutional fact.**
- Item 7 (the *Vigil* tender's days on station) — **confirmed, with the interpolation base now stated.**
- Item 8 (ordinary work landward of the third seam) — **confirmed.**
- Items 9–13 as numbered in the Batch 0004 and Batch 0005 blocks — **all ruled.** The chapter-length records are settled and the false figure is withdrawn.
- The two older blocks of "deliberate soft points" and "Batch 0001 review repairs" remain above as history and are superseded by the audit where they conflict with it.

**The correction that mattered most, recorded here so it is not lost:** `state/continuity.md` asserted *twenty-four marks of air bought since the twenty-eighth of October*. **No chapter said twenty-four and the chapters are right.** The prose gives eleven bought in the week of the fifteenth of October (Ch 16), nine by the fourth of November (Ch 30), four on the thirteenth (Ch 37), five in the three-day mole job of the sixteenth to the eighteenth (Ch 42) and eleven on the first of December (Ch 46) — **thirteen as at the twenty-eighth of November, which is the figure Chapter 45 reads out in the hearing room and which closes mark-for-mark from Chapters 30 and 37 alone, and twenty-nine by the first of December.** The figure is corrected everywhere it was repeated below.

## Batch 0005 as written (Chapters 41–50) — binding on Volume 02, and the close of Volume 01

### Calendar

- **Fri 18th, Sat 19th, Sun 20th, Mon 21st, Tue 22nd, Wed 23rd, Thu 24th, Fri 25th, Sat 26th, Sun 27th, Mon 28th, Tue 29th, Wed 30th** of November. Then **Thu 1st, Fri 2nd, Sat 3rd, Sun 4th, Mon 5th, Tue 6th, Wed 7th, Thu 8th, Fri 9th, Sat 10th, Sun 11th, Mon 12th, Tue 13rd, Wed 14th, Thu 15th, Fri 16th, Sat 17th, Sun 18th, Mon 19th, Tue 20th, Wed 21st, Thu 22nd, Fri 23rd, Sat 24th, Sun 25th, Mon 26th** of December. Then **Tue 1st of January**, and so **Sun 1st, Mon 2nd, Tue 3rd, Wed 4th** of January, and **Thu 19th of January**.
- **The weekdays follow Batch 0003 and Batch 0004 and are not negotiable:** 14 November was a Monday, so 21 and 28 are Mondays, 22 is a Tuesday, 23 a Wednesday, and 1 December is a Thursday, so **6 December is a Tuesday, 8 December a Thursday, 10 December a Saturday, 13 December a Tuesday, 17 December a Saturday, 20 December a Tuesday, 21 December a Wednesday, 25 December a Sunday, and 26 December a Monday.** **1 January is a Sunday, 4 January is a Wednesday, 19 January is a Thursday, and 1 March is a Wednesday** — which is what makes 26 December to 1 March exactly **nine weeks and two days, eighty-one days**, and makes Chapter 50's *six days before the New Year* checkable. *(The audit corrected the claim once made here that Chapter 48's *eleven weeks and a day* to the first of March was also checkable. It was not: the true figure from the tenth of December is **eleven weeks and four days**, the chapter has been repaired in place, and the claim about that phrase has been removed rather than restated.)* Every weekday spoken in Chapters 41–50 was checked against that grid and corrected in two repair passes: the first pass had the December dates one day out in four places and the filing of the supplementary paper on a Monday that does not exist in the month, and the second pass corrected **17 December, 20 December and 25 December, which the first grid stated wrongly**, together with the residual slips the Batch 0005 review found in Chapters 41, 42, 43, 44, 46, 47, 48 and 49.
- **The harbor authority's clerk wipes the hearing room's slate wall every Monday at eight**, and the wall is therefore blank at the sittings of the 21st (no sitting, no wall) and the 28th of November, and was last wiped on **Monday the nineteenth of December**; the hearing room of the twenty-first is a Tuesday and the wall had been standing three days, and it is wiped again on the twenty-sixth. The ledger is the record.
- **The *Vigil*'s tender's days on station are carried forward from the page and are not computed from the calendar** (the standing reading lock): **thirty-nine on the 23rd of November, forty-four on the 28th, seventy-two on the 26th of December** — the only three figures on the page. A day-count for any other date must be interpolated at one a day from these and **must not be stated as though a chapter had given it.** A second cutter's boat, not in Morrow Quay in October, is at the naval pile from December.
- **Kest writes a Sunday entry on the 27th of November**, which he says he has never done in fifteen years and which the office keeps no hours for. **The chart claim is narrowed on the 28th** and the returned services begin **on the first of December**.

### The purpose, the supplement, and the hearing of the 28th

1. **The purpose for the sitting of the twenty-eighth**, filed on the 21st in Calder Marr's own left hand, read into the record with the door shut on the 22nd. Six pages and about a thousand words. It asks for a one-season provisional working charter; it names what it does **not** ask for in nine lines (the notice of the 31st, the four-hundred-yard bar, the fifth seam, the custody room, anything outside the harbor line, and the Second Bell license); it states the three points with the ten yards withdrawn and the first point entered as a bedded seam of about forty feet; it promises a stated reason, a named tender who is not the diver, a cutoff, and a book four men can read; and **it does not state why the yard wants the ground.**
2. **Kest's entry of the 22nd, in the margin of his own book, which the clerk does not copy:** that the purpose of the 21st does not name the ground and the holder states that he does not know it. He tells Calder he has done it and is not proud of it.
3. **The harbor authority's own account of its own works**, read in the barge on the 27th with the clerk in the room and not copied: the mole was carried out to the southward by about fifty yards in the spring of the year of the Cut, of stone from the north and material from the foreshore at the south, and a surveyor's hand wrote *the foreshore at the south end being mixed, containing iron and timber*. **The mole was built on the ferry.**
4. **The supplementary paper**, four hundred and six words, filed at ten past ten on the morning of the 28th, an hour before the room fills. It states that the purpose of the 21st is complete as to what the yard asks and silent as to the ground, that the silence is not a withholding, that a party which files a purpose on a Monday and asks for the bottom on a Saturday six days later is a party which ought to be asked about, and it asks to be put in the water. **The filing moved from the 30th to the morning of the 28th** so that the yard's admission would be in the reeve's hand before the reeve opened his book.
5. **The one-season provisional working charter**, granted in the hearing room on Monday the 28th of November, to run **to the last day of March next**, on harbor ground inside the harbor line, bounded by the outer marks and by four hundred yards off them. It permits the holder to work any wreck or object on the authority's ground inside the line and to be put in the water for that work under a permit, on a bond, on dates named in advance by the reeve. **It is expressly not the Second Bell license and does not lead to one.** **The harbor's eighth and the harbor's share of any raising follow the yard in** and were not argued. **The crew's own share is retained** at the figure the crews of the association had already agreed. **The bond is the freed shoal iron** at the clerk's valuation of thirteen to the crew, with a third entered to the *Low Lantern*. **It does not reach the fifth seam, does not lift, narrow, re-serve or alter the notice of the 31st, and does not reach the third clause of it.** It **may be suspended by this office at any time after an inspection, for cause**, and Kest says so out loud on the 26th of December, says he has no cause, and says he will not suspend it.
6. **The reeve's undertaking of the 25th of October is lifted** for the term of the charter and no longer, and only on dates named by the reeve, and only with a stated reason entered in the reeve's book before the diver goes in, a named tender who is not the diver, a cutoff, and a book four men can read the same day it is written. **This is Calder Marr's own undertaking and he did not ask for it.**
7. **Kest's price, on his own motion and in his own voice:** the chart claim on the mole's inner face is **narrowed from the third-to-eleventh seams to three seams about the fifth** (the fifth, and the fourth and the sixth). Consequence, in the order it bites: the timber fenders all come back into service on the first of December; **three of the four sets of moorings** come back; **one of the two herring berths** comes back; **the ninth seam's mooring stays out** because a fouled shackle bedded in stone is not a seam and clause three of a notice the reeve did not write holds the man who would work it; **the north herring berth stays out**; and **the nineteen winters of dues remain disputed and unpaid**. Neale, foreman of the tide gang, is in the room and is answered in the reeve's own voice rather than by an order.
8. **Kest's fortnight's promise to Elias Voke is paid on the 28th**, which is the day he promised. The coast office has answered with four lines: it acknowledges the application, notes the north berth will be out on the first of March, and requires **a statement of the authority's capacity to moor the fleet** before it will consider a hard at Ashtide. Voke says the word *capacity* is not an answer, says he will write the statement himself for nothing, and says he will put the north berth, the ninth seam and the boy's name in it. **The hard at Ashtide is unanswered and is carried into Volume 2.**
9. **The hearing record of the 28th** contains, in addition: the purpose and the supplement and the admission of the silence; the yard's statement that it asks to be put in the water with Pike on the cleat; the notice that the six clauses of the reeve's dead instrument of the 3rd were good and the countersign was missing; the *Vigil's* terms answered; the accounts read out in full; the reeve's price; the reeve's undertaking lifted; **the schedule of three named dates, the sixth, the eighth and the tenth of December, with Bevin Rook as the diver on the tenth and Calder Marr on the tender's cleat and Pike on the fall**; and the *Vigil*'s tender moving to a cable's length.

### What the crew found in the water off the south end of the mole

- **The claim window of the 27th of November** names the mole's face at the south end **beyond the eleventh seam** and the water off it inside the harbor line, for one date, for a look, with a hand lead and a slate. **The notice of the 31st closes the third to the eleventh seams only, and has never closed water.** This is the volume's answer to *how a man gets a steel on that ground* and it is a form from a man in a barge.
- **The plate.** Ferry iron, three-eighths, lying on the new mole's rubble slope in **about nine and a quarter feet** at the mole face, with the depth **eight and a quarter inside the coaming** by a lead off a boat and not better than a foot on either figure. It is **not glass and it is not the plate in the custody room** and it has nothing to do with the transparent keel on the shoal. It is the *Aster Gannet*'s own iron.
- **The coaming.** Four foot by three foot **outside**, measured from a boat on the 27th; **three foot nine by two foot four clear inside**, measured with a steel rule in the hand on the 8th of December. The bellwright's reading: a stores hatch, not a crew's hatch; a crew's hatch is two foot square.
- **The grating.** Out of its seat and in its seat, the rebate worn bright on the **inboard side only**, a stowage board in it that is not on the survey, and a hasp broken at the stem. The survey is the ferry's own and gives the grating in place before she sailed.
- **The aperture.** Low on the outboard side, a hand's breadth and a half, edges dressed with a bevel on both sides, **a circle of fourteen small holes at an even spacing**, half-inch iron, cut to a template from outside. **The fouling in the holes and on the bevel is continuous with the fouling on the plate outside and gives no hour, no man and no direction.** Nobody in the crew has touched it, nobody can say who cut it or when or from where, and it may have been cut from the water or from the deck and no instrument on this coast will tell it.
- **The measurement and its exact shape, which is the volume's reversal and not its climax.** The survey gives standing space number four, port side aft, as **eight foot four by six foot nine clear and eleven feet two deep under the grating**; the manifest says the hold stood empty. The measured opening is three foot nine by two foot four. The space lies under a grating in a deck, so **the hatch is the only way anything has ever gone into that space or come out of it**, and nothing of the case's seven-to-eight-foot described size could pass it in or out, so **the space cannot have been cleared**; and this office's own thirteen-year-old file of the wreck, in a harbor's hand, gives that space as *number four, port side aft, grating in place, **not cleared, contents not removed***, which is a harbor saying in writing that the space stood uncleared and that it was never cleared on any record here. So a paper saying the hold stood empty at the moment of the seizure is not describing the space at that moment whatever else is true. The yard wanted to prove the room was occupied and has proved the room cannot have been cleared, which is worth more because it is a figure and not a sentence. **What stands in the room is still unknown and is not asked for.** **Note the order of the argument, because the Batch 0005 review found the chapter had it the other way round:** the measurement excludes *this case* through *this opening* and does not by itself establish that the space was never cleared of anything else. The missing step — that the hatch is the only opening, and that the harbor's own file records the space as not cleared — is now in Chapter 47 on the page, and it must be carried into any later volume that repeats the finding.
- **The stern board.** Six foot with about two foot remaining, rabbetted on the back, three letters under the fouling at the broken end, *A*, *s* and most of a *t*. It came up on the fall with the core on the 6th and was not entered until the 7th, and the boy who had it for two days says so in the book.
- **The fragment.** A hand's breadth and a half of worked glass in two keyed portions, three fingers thick at the fat edge, with **a band ground flat the whole length of the lower cut face, curved**, the worked lines running down into the band and stopping at its edge. It came off the plate's outboard edge below the aperture. The yard enters **the form** of the band, quotes the conservator's words of the second of October underneath it, states that **no person in this harbor has compared this object with the object in the custody room**, and will not draw a line between them. The two objects stay separate objects.

### The bell-buoy core, as a working thing and not a device

- Recovered on **Tuesday the 6th of December** in **forty-one minutes** at the top of the flood, in about eight and a half feet, fouled off in slabs. Iron, forged, a hand's breadth and a half, flat foot, curved back, a forged yoke and a shackle, a clapper on a gudgeon with **the tongue half worn through**, and **four pieces of worked glass leaded in a band in the waist** with lead gone white.
- **It answers.** A bell in the ground answers another bell in the same ground through the keel-line between them, and the answer arrives **a hand's width of a second late**. This is the whole of the volume's motif: the delayed note has come up off the mole since Chapter 1 and has arrived with every iron in the harbor — the tide bell, the mole's chain alarm of the 28th of October, the buoy's clapper of the 20th of October — and the crew had never once struck anything.
- **The craft, as the bellwright states it and as the chapter obeys:** the sound is the iron, and the glass in the waist is not decoration, it is what makes a bell a *signal* instead of a noise, and nobody on this coast knows why and nobody claims to. **The trade is the going out**: fouling is not the enemy, a wet gudgeon writes the bell off, water under the lead clouds four pieces of glass for good, and a bell that is not looked after stops answering and starts being a rock. A new tongue is a day's work every four or five years for as long as it hangs.
- **It cannot be worked in a shed.** The second clause of the notice of the 31st keeps recovered transparent mineral out of every shed in the harbor, the yard has never argued it, and therefore **the core is hung or it rusts.** The bellwright will not open a bell in the wet. Nothing was refitted.
- **It is hung** on Sunday the 25th of December on a harbor shackle, a harbor chain and a short moor, on the authority's own ground line, in eight and a half feet off the south end, with a chart notation in Kest's own hand: *maintained by the Free Keel yard, not a mark and not a light and not a danger, a signal kept by hand, at the cost stated in this office's hearing record of the twenty-eighth of November.* It has not been taken off and will not be. **The twenty-fifth was a Sunday; the Batch 0005 review corrected the chapter from Saturday.**

### The fragment, the entry, and the cost

- **Entered under the charter and the permit of the 8th of December, on the Free Keel platform in three fathoms inside the mole** — not on the mole's face, because a yard with a boy on a sling could not take a bell entry on a wall in eight and a half feet with a swell round the corner, and Nessa Pike said so out loud and was right, and the reason for the platform was entered under the permit and read out at the slip.
- **All six conditions, read aloud and said with her:** named tender Pike; an independent line; a fixed air limit of forty minutes on the slate in two hands; a seated named weight; the cutoff *Enough*; and a stated reason entered in the harbor authority's book at eight in the morning, unedited, with the clerk present.
- **The anchor is the *Aster Gannet's* own stern board** and not Oren's brass rigger's hand bell, in Calder's words: the bell is his brother's and will not go into a public record on the eighth of December, and the ship was in the room and is not a person. **The bell is never named in any record and never goes into a shed.**
- **The scene, and it is one witness's limited frame.** Low on a loading space with a case on the shoulder, four men on it and the witness one of them. **The man with the paper was behind him** and the paper was upside down and no letters were legible; a phrase with one word in it was read twice and the word was *empty*; the case went past the man's knees between the two readings and he did not look down; the witness counted four and the count was right; a lead tag lay on the line at his feet and he had the first two figures and lost the third; **a hand was put on the collar at the back of it, not rough and not gentle, and moved him, and he went looking at the man with the paper and not at the case**, and the man turned the paper over. A part of the scene would not resolve: a doubled edge with a hard rim. **No name is entered and none has been asked for.**
- **It does not speak a command, apologize, forgive, or explain anything**, and the evacuation bell and the second rescue line are not in it and are reserved to the Volume 15 midpoint.
- **Eleven minutes inside a limit of forty**, surfaced on the tender's word, and the aftertaste came out in Oren's call mark on the boards, and Nessa Pike went white and held a mooring post at the far end of the platform.
- **The cost, and it is in the body.** The morning of the 9th: Calder Marr can remember the call, its length, its three marks, its flick, and the place in the middle where a boy's breath went, and **the lift at the end of it is not there and there is nothing to remember.** It is not muffled and not forgotten; it is absent. **No person in Morrow Quay can restore it and none will be asked.**
- **The price of the public route, said once and in the open by Nessa Pike:** a boy on a deck in a public record with no name on him is the only way anybody will ever know he was there, and is also the only reason nobody will ever know which boy. Calder takes it knowingly; the private alternative was refused in his own hand on the 3rd of November with three reasons.
- **The fragment is in the Morrow circuit custody room**, entered on the 13th of December as the **third** item under a third number, *a fragment of undetermined origin, not compared by this office with any other item*, under the same seal line as every other paper the circuit office has sent to Morrow Quay since the 14th of October. **A municipal claim is entered against it for the purpose of a record and not for the purpose of possession**, the yard is not entered as a custodian, and **the claim cannot be withdrawn without a request, and the yard will not make one.** The three numbers' digits are still not quoted in the prose.
- **The concealment holds at the end of the volume.** The mark is in no record, in no office, in no book, and not in Kest's chart notation. It is in the mouths of two people: Calder's and Mara's, and Mara's is not written anywhere. Bevin Rook heard something come out of a man's mouth on the eighth of December and wrote *not written as a voice* and has said aloud in the yard that he will not write it and was not asked. **Nessa Pike knows there was something and has not asked.** On the 26th of December, in a grown man's voice, out of the hung core, with a lift on the end of it, a call comes up through the ground, and Calder cannot place it and does not turn round.
- **The one false line on the unverified note is left false.** It says the personal association was confirmed by the diver and not disclosed. Two words would make it true. He puts the slate back on its own shelf with the false line in it, because a true slate with a boy's name on it is a document in a yard any man may read.

### The delayed note, as it now stands

- **Heard and logged across Volume 01: heard many times, logged eight** (the 26th, 2nd, 4th of November, the 8th, the 14th, the 23rd, the 6th and the 26th of December), with the 8th of November shared with a naval rating's slate and the 6th of December being the first occasion the sound came out of an object in the crew's own hands rather than out of the bottom. Two occasions went unwritten, on the 28th of October (the mole's chain alarm) and the 29th of October (the top of the make with no book in anyone's hand). **It is not resolved and Bevin Rook's book is never going to resolve it, and the whole of the answer inside the note is reserved to the Volume 15 midpoint.**
- **It has a cause and the cause is a bell.** Every logged occasion is an iron in the harbor being struck: the tide bell, the chain alarm, the buoy's clapper. The core in the water under the south end answered the tide bell on the 6th and on the 26th with the same delay. **The mole was built on the ferry; the run is continuous under the town.** Nobody can say what is in the answer.
- **The 26th of December's entry has one line more than the others:** *knock, delayed, with a call in it. Three marks and a lift. Adult. Not written as a voice. Nobody in this yard has said whose it is.*

### The three points, as they now stand

- **The difference of ten yards is withdrawn by the yard. What is on no record and on no wall is the difference stated as a measurement; what is on a wall is the withdrawal itself**, on a fresh market-wall sheet under the Navy's paper and on the yard's own three sheets with the interval taken off them. **The first point is not a point**: it is a bedded seam of transparent material in the mole's own foundation running north under the rubble to about two boat lengths, of which the yard used the south end as a position, and the yard has said so itself in print.
- **What the yard can now add without contradicting the ruling that no line may be drawn:** a fourth place, on the same bearing, where an iron bell answers the harbor's own tide bell through the ground, with a delay that is a figure in a book's own hand, and a fifth, the plate in nine and a quarter feet off the south end, and a sixth, the mole's own works account naming iron and timber in the foot of a mole built in the year the ferry went down. **No line is drawn between any of them and none is to be.**
- **The new-volume question, opened and not closed, in Sera Mott's own words on the 21st of December:** the transparent vessels lie in a deliberate route and are not scattered; there was more of them out there; this one has been heard answering from the westward and always late; the ones that answered from the westward were kept by men who went out in boats, and **the ones she knows about stopped, because the men who kept them stopped going out, and the men stopped because there was nobody on the far banks by the time anybody asked.** She has been asked by archivists and has said no and believed them. **That is a road with nobody on it and not a mystery.** The seaward end of the run is under a naval claim with a tender on it at a cable's length, and nobody has ever asked the cutter what the seaward end is for. **Volume 2 and after carry this.**

### Documents and instruments created in Batch 0005

1. The purpose of the 21st, six pages, and the reeve's margin entry of the 22nd.
2. The harbor authority's own account of its own works, read in the barge on the 27th, not copied.
3. The claim window of the 27th, one date, a look, a hand lead and a slate.
4. The reeve's Sunday-night entry of the 27th and the copy given to the yard.
5. The supplementary paper of the 28th, four hundred and six words, filed at ten past ten.
6. The hearing record of the sitting of the 28th, with the purpose, the supplement, the admission, the charter, the price, Voke's answer, the accounts, the lifting of the undertaking, and the schedule of three dates.
7. **The one-season provisional working charter of the 28th**, with the bond, the eighth, the share, the limits, and the suspension clause.
8. The reeve's chart claim **as narrowed** on the 28th, to three seams about the fifth, with the cost entered.
9. The *Vigil*'s paper of the 23rd and the yard's answer of the 23rd, on the market wall, in four sentences, dated and houred on the back, with the six-line count.
10. **The count of the ten printed crew copies**, in Bevin Rook's right hand and Nessa Pike's countersign, six lines including the line about other crews' paper, and Mara Venn's certification of *method* for nothing, with *nothing* written on the back in Calder's hand.
11. The crew's book entries: the purpose's incompleteness (21st), Alden's absence (19th), **the mole job (16th–18th)**, the tender's answer and the count (23rd), **the coaming and aperture (27th)**, the core (6th), the stern board (7th), the purpose for the entry (8th), the cost (9th), Rook's return to the water (10th), the fragment's entry (13th), the money read out (13th), **the wages word taken out (14th)**, the chart notation read out (25th), the answer of the 26th.
12. **The public sheet of the 17th of December — a Saturday, corrected in the review pass** — three parts in four hands, with the limits written inside the rules, on the market wall under the cutter's claim, and six copies carried to the herring skippers, the net loft and the *Low Lantern*, and none printed.
13. **The custody-log entry of the 20th of December — a Tuesday, corrected in the review pass** — re-entering three items to *municipal claim entered, contested*, under the seal line, with no reason.
14. **The chart notation of the 25th of December** for the hung core, in the reeve's own hand.
15. The **claim entered against the third custody item** on the 13th of December, for the purpose of a record and not of possession, and the statement that it cannot be withdrawn without a request.

### People

- **Alden Marr, 61. Falls silent.** He was warm on the 13th of November and was not in the yard from the Saturday the 19th, and sent word that it is the cough and that he is not to be disturbed and not to be sent for. He is at the far bench in the same shed. He is entered in the crew's book as *not chased, no reason given and none asked for*, and Calder does not go. **He has not spoken to anybody since the 13th of November and is not asked for anything in Chapters 41–50.** No confession, no fragment, no report talk, nothing about the ferry, and the plate note untouched.
- **Bevin Rook, 22.** Left hand a fortnight to three weeks along, cannot close, left arm in the sling, right hand writes. **Back in the water on Saturday the 10th of December at nineteen minutes with his hand in his pocket, and tendered by Calder Marr with Nessa Pike on the fall, and pulled by *Enough*.** He is owed **forty marks** — sixteen for the reading from the 14th of October and twenty-four for the hand from the 29th of October — and the reading is a trade until the tenth and the wages begin at four a week on the tenth and are not paid. **Nessa Pike takes the word *wages* out of the yard's book on Wednesday the 14th of December** — the day after the money was read out on the Tuesday the 13th — **and puts *claim* in its place, in her own hand, because he asked.** He asks the volume's last question of a private kind on the 13th: whether the forty is a wage or a claim, and answers his own question better than anyone answers it for him. He is the man who found the core from the mole steps, who read the whole of the purpose's defect, who added the sixth line to the count, who read out the reason for the entry to the tide gang, who wrote *not written as a voice*, who entered the third *not stated* of a thing he promised not to enter, and who read the year's work back at the end of it.
- **Nessa Pike, 27.** Fifth overrule of the campaign and the fifth she wins: Calder's total refusal of the *Vigil*'s paper on the 23rd, which she replaces with *grant one, grant three, refuse two, and grant first so the refusal is believed*. **Her cost is the count**, signed in her own hand, of ten printed copies and their places — the thing she said in a shed could be bent by a man with a notebook. Her second cost is the hardest word in the batch: *Enough*, to a boy of twenty-two she has kept out of the water for six weeks, whom she does not apologize to, and the money read out on the 13th with *wages* in it that she takes out on the 14th because he asked. She overrules the mole's face as the entry site. She still holds the tender's cleat, the pump, the fall, the board and the slate, and the standing shutdown is unchanged.
- **Ors Kest, 44.** Lifts his own undertaking for a season without being asked. Grants a charter that is a leash and says so. **Pays for it in one herring berth and a mooring on the ninth seam and nineteen winters of dues that are not paid**, and says the price in a hearing room and again to a man in the road. Writes a Sunday entry he has never written, puts the yard's own incompleteness into his own book before the hearing, and keeps a copy of that entry out of the circuit office and out of the *Vigil*'s sight. Refuses the *Vigil*'s request for the harbor authority's own chart in eleven seconds and says why, and records that the yard has no right to ask for it and has not asked. Names the nineteenth of January after Calder asks, not before. **He has now spent authority he did not plan to spend four times in a month and every one of the four cost somebody other than the yard a berth, a moor, a rope or a hearing.**
- **Mara Venn, 30.** Four *not stated* entries, three private and one public, and the query on her certificate expense comes off her file on **the fourth of January**, after which she has no standing to refuse anything. She certifies a public method for nothing and has the yard write *nothing* on the back. She refuses to witness the reason for the entry, refuses to say whether the mark is written, and in the last scene refuses to say out loud the thing she has believed since the fourteenth of October, because a thing she said would make her a witness and she would rather be the only person who knows what is in that anchor and have never been asked. **She is not thanked and does not want to be and says so twice.**
- **Elias Voke, 51.** Nineteen winters of dues disputed and unpaid, one berth back, the ninth seam's mooring out, the coast office's word *capacity*, and a promise to write the authority's capacity statement himself and put the boy's name in it. **Carried into Volume 2 whole.**
- **Neale**, foreman of the harbor's tide gang, is in the room on the 28th and is answered in the reeve's own voice. **Sella Brigg** carries a copy of the public sheet and writes the date on the back of her own copy in her own hand; her third of the iron is entered on the charter's bond and still to be paid in February. **Harl Dace** reads the sheet at the net loft. **Sera Mott**, 68, says the volume's new question out loud at a mole head and asks for nothing to be written. **Tobias Wren, 61**, supplies the mole's history, takes the boat, and says the thing about the bell that the bellwright also says: the going out is the whole of it.

### The batch's money chain, which Volume 02 must not break

- Start on the 13th of November: **five marks in the bag, nothing in the tin**, the note at six paid and still written up in the chandler's own book as **eleven weeks into six**.
- **16th–18th, mole work, Thursday to Saturday, the first and second seams, no diver, no boat, no pump:** thirty-four on the board, paid in the road on the Saturday. Out: **the harbor's eighth 3, the harbor's share 3, rope and the day's food 3, five marks of air, six on the chandler's note** — twenty out. **Six on the note in those three days and none since, which is thirteen weeks by the yard's count and eleven in the chandler's book, and neither figure has been offered to the chandler.** Bag **19**.
- **23rd:** three marks for Mara Venn's certified copy of a hearing record, which is a certificate about a record and not about glass, **entered against the nineteen and paid at the copying room on the first of December**, which is what Chapter 46's arithmetic depends on.
- **1st of December:** eleven marks of air, and the three marks for the certified copy. The crane and the barge are the harbor's and the harbor pays the hire, four a day for three days, on the authority's own account, on the authority's own ground. **19 − 3 − 11 = 5.** Calder refuses to put one mark on the chandler's note in a week he cannot afford.
- **The bag holds five from the first of December to the end of the volume, and nothing has gone into it since the eighteenth of November. The tin has been empty all batch.** The chandler's note is still written up in his book as eleven weeks into six. **The chandler has never been asked and neither has Calder.** *(The Batch 0005 review found this line claiming the bag was **nil** from the second of December, which contradicted Chapter 46's own line 13 and Chapter 50's line 119. It is five, and it is five on the sixth and on the twenty-sixth.)*
- **Forty marks owed to Rook, forty to Venn, none of it paid.** The freed shoal iron is unsold on the stack at twenty marks with a third entered to Brigg for February, and is now entered as the charter's bond at the clerk's valuation of thirteen to the crew. The pump is cracked. The *Kittiwake* is on the hard with her bilge blocked in and no cradle, and was shifted down to the slip for two tides in the first week of December by three men and a hand-barrow because there was a bell to come up, and went back. **November's slip tides are gone and December's were not asked for.**
- **The charter is paper and not money.** There is nothing to draw on until something is raised, and nothing has been raised, and the harbor's eighth comes off the top of anything they ever raise.

### Costs the volume may not waive, now closed and carried

- **Money:** thirty-four marks of gross work in three days in November and twenty out of it; **five marks of air in those three days and eleven on the first of December**; three marks for a certified copy, paid on the first; **six on the chandler's note in the three days of that job and none since**; two harbor hires paid by the authority; forty marks owed to a boy of twenty-two and forty to a conservator, both unpaid, **on a bag that has held five marks since the first of December and has had nothing put into it since the eighteenth of November.** **A poor crew has not been rescued and the charter does not rescue it.**
- **Body:** Bevin Rook's hand, three weeks along, in a pocket on a cradle in eight feet on the tenth of December; Calder's forearm ungloved and re-enacting on the ninth; the aftertaste of the eighth; **and a specific memory gone, permanently, which is the price of a public route and which no office and no person in Morrow Quay can restore.**
- **Standing:** two custody objects still gone, a third gone on the 13th of December, a chart claim narrowed from eight seams to three, a notice that still closes eight seams and cannot be lifted, a bar off the outer marks that still stands, a reeve's instrument still conditional and its condition still failed, and **the reason for the seizure of the 14th of October on no paper this harbor has ever been handed.**
- **Time:** two purposes, a supplement, four hearing-room sittings in one month, three days of mole work, three permitted diving days, and a boy in the water on a date fixed in a record.
- **Authority:** Calder admitted in a public record that his own purpose was incomplete, six days before a sitting, of his own motion. He gave the boy his name to nobody. He left the false line in his own slate.

## Volume 01 close accounting — as AUDITED

*Audited 26 September 2026 against the fifty chapters. This supersedes the batch's own account of itself, which appears above and which was in several places the account of a plan rather than of a page. The audit is `reviews/volume-01/volume-01-close.md`.*

**Answered by Volume 01, and answered on the page.** The provenance of the first recovery is a chain of labor no office has disturbed. The *Vigil's* claim and the recovered plate rest on the same ground, and the plate was lifted two days before the claim was lodged, and **no forum has ruled on either.** The public manifest is **unreliable as a description of standing space number four, in three steps and in that order:** the clear opening is three foot nine by two foot four, the space is eight foot four by six foot nine clear and eleven feet two deep under the grating, the case is seven to eight feet in its longest face, **the hatch is the only opening**, so the space cannot have been cleared; **and this office's own thirteen-year-old file, in a harbor's hand, says *grating in place, not cleared, contents not removed*.** A working bell-buoy core is recovered, understood as a signal and not a relic, hung on harbor ground at a real cost, and it answers the harbor's own tide bell through the ground **a hand's width of a second late** — the same second as the sixth of December, said aloud by the boy with the book. A fragment holding the first minutes of the seizure is recovered and surfaced through a public route **at the cost of a specific and permanent childhood memory of Oren's voice**, entered in custody under a claim the yard cannot withdraw and will not ask to withdraw. The one-season provisional working charter is granted to the last day of March, the salvage share is kept, the eighth and the harbor's share follow the yard in, the reeve's undertaking is lifted on named dates, and it is not the Second Bell and does not lead to one. Bevin Rook is in the water on the tenth of December under a tender who is not him. The crew is still poor, owes a boy forty marks and a conservator forty marks, and has a chandler's note nobody has re-written. **Ilya Serr's name and senior Registry examiner's seal stand on a seal line and never in a room, and the reason for the seizure is still undisclosed.** Calder still hides the Oren mark at the volume boundary, and the concealment is now three things: a false line on his own slate he has declined to correct, a boy with a book who said out loud in the yard that he will not write what came out of a man's mouth and was not asked, and a woman with no standing left who knows what is in the anchor and has never been asked to say it.

**Opened by Volume 01.** Why the transparent vessels lie in a deliberate route toward Orison and who holds the missing links — and the second half, opened by Sera Mott at a mole head on the twenty-first of December: there was more of them, this one has been heard answered from the westward and always late, the ones that answered from the westward were kept by men who went out in boats, **and the ones she knows about stopped, because the men who kept them stopped going out, and the men stopped because there was nobody on the far banks by the time anybody asked.** It is a road with nobody on it and not a mystery. Why a presumption of Crown material expired in December and nothing arrived. Who cut a hole in a ferry's shell from the outside, and when, and from where. What a builder's mark of nine joined lines means and no office in Veyr can say. What a cutter is doing with a tender at a cable's length on the seaward end of a run of glass. Whether a route whose maintenance stopped can be restarted by people who have never agreed to keep it.

**Owed to Volume 02, and named, and now planned.** `outline/volume-02.md` carries all of it as locks and `outline/batches/volume-02-batch-0001.md` places it across Chapters 51–60: **Elias Voke's statement of the authority's capacity to moor the fleet, the hard at Ashtide, and the first of March — a Wednesday, nine weeks and two days off — with one herring berth short and the ninth seam's mooring out.** The nineteenth of January, a Thursday, at the mole's toe, with a lead and a slate and a bell hung and no diver unless a reason is in the reeve's book at eight in the morning with Pike on the cleat, **and the run traced inland, under the town, where the harbor authority has no chart because the town was built on it.** The circuit office's next request, worded so a refusal is a delay, which is the instrument of Mara Venn's Volume 4 break and may not break in Volume 2. The forty marks, twice, and the first four weeks falling in January and February, and Sella Brigg's third in February. The nineteen winters of dues. A charter that can be suspended after an inspection by one man with a key and a book. A notice that cannot be lifted in Morrow Quay. Two proper plans for the fifth seam, owed to Volumes 2 and 3, and the mechanism is a change of politics in an office and not a better argument from a shed. A coast that is poor and busy and short of a hard. A bell with a fouled waist and a tongue half worn through, hung on the town's own ground, that somebody has to go out and look after.

## Rulings of the Volume 01 volume audit — 26 September 2026

**Every author-level decision five batches deferred is now ruled, not deferred again.** The full reasoning is in `reviews/volume-01/volume-01-close.md` Part 2, and each ruling is mirrored in `state/open-threads.md`.

1. **Ordinary harbor work landward of the third seam is outside the notice's closure** (former item 8). **CONFIRMED.** The notice closes the mole's toe and inner face from the third seam to the eleventh, does not reach the twelfth, the dump, or water, and **no notice has ever closed water.** Clause three catches only the authority's work. A crew the notice bars may still take private work on harbor ground inside the line. **This is the only reason the yard has money in Volume 1 and it stays true in Volume 2.**
2. **The *Vigil* tender's days on station are carried forward from the page** (former item 7). **CONFIRMED, and the base is now stated:** interpolate **from the fourth of November at twenty days**, which reproduces every figure on the page exactly — twenty-four on the eighth, thirty-nine on the twenty-third, forty-four on the twenty-eighth, seventy-two on the twenty-sixth of December. **Never state a day-count no chapter gave as though a chapter had given it.**
3. **The hearing room's slate wall is wiped every Monday at eight** (former item 6). **CONFIRMED** as an institutional fact. Figures on that wall survive one week; the ledger is the record and the wall is a convenience.
4. **Chapter 38 tells Mara Venn the Oren mark** where the Batch 0004 card asked for the hiding to continue (former item 9). **RULED: THE CHAPTER STANDS AND THE CARD IS AMENDED PROSPECTIVELY.** The volume closed with the mark still hidden from every office, from Kest and from his father, so `outline/volume-01.md`'s requirement is met; and two of the concealment's three parts exist only because Chapter 38 happened. **Binding:** a concealment may be told once, to one person, in a private scene, at a price; it may not be told a second time for nothing; the volume closes with it hidden. **The deviation is accepted, not tolerated, and is no longer a proposed canon change.**
5. **The bellwright's core and the craft of it** (former item 10). **RULED: THE BIBLE ALREADY PERMITS IT. ITEM CLOSED. NO AMENDMENT PROPOSED.** `bible/terminology.md` defines a *bell buoy* as a buoy that can *listen for a stable echo*, and the core listened and answered; `bible/power-system.md`'s Bellwright entry is a craft and not a rank, and a harbor's own tradesman is not a stage. The *answer inside the note* stays reserved to the Volume 15 midpoint, as `outline/ending.md` promise 1 already reserves it. **The six bible files are unchanged.** **The words *it reads nothing, it settles nothing and it says no word* are the volume's lock and not a sentence in any chapter**; the negative is carried by the craft's economics and by Kest's chart notation, and a later volume must keep the object on that footing rather than hunt for a line to quote.
6. **The *Aster Gannet's* wreck lies in pieces under and beside the south end of the mole, and the mole was built on her in the year she went down** (former item 11). **RATIFIED as canon.** It is the only lawful ground on which the compartment could be measured inside the harbor line, and without it there is no plate, no coaming, no grating, no aperture, no fragment and no midpoint. No custody object returns; nothing on claimed ground is touched; the *Gannet's* ferry iron and the transparent keel are two objects and are never fused.
7. **The midpoint's exact finding** (former item 12). **RULED: THE THREE-STEP FORM IN CHAPTER 47 IS RATIFIED**, and is carried into `outline/volume-02.md` as **lock L6**, with the instruction that **any later volume that restates the finding restates all three steps and never the two-step form.** The finding is **the space cannot have been cleared**; it proves the manifest unreliable and proves nothing whatever about what was in the space; and **no chapter may state or imply that the sealed case was in standing space number four.** The three Gannet documents stay three documents. `outline/ending.md` promise 4 pays this in Volume 15 and a volume that compresses the chain between here and there makes the finale's payoff smaller than its setup.
8. **The bellwright is unnamed** (former item 13). **RULED: HE IS NAMED IN VOLUME 02 AND THE NAME IS CORVIN SARN**, the harbor's own bellwright of Morrow Quay, about fifty, with a blacksmith's forearms, in that yard since the twentieth of October. **The name lands in Movement 1, in the chapter that carries the first of the going out — Chapter 55 — and it is spoken in a person's mouth and not written in a narrator's list.** **He teaches nobody**, he is the harbor's bellwright and not the Free Keel's, and he is not a Stage 5 mentor.
9. **The two length records** (former items 14 and 7). **THE CLAIM THAT BATCH 0005 WAS WORSE ON LENGTH THAN BATCH 0004 IS WITHDRAWN.** Batch 0005 is **35,514 words against Batch 0004's 39,536** and past the 3,200 guideline in seven chapters against nine; the 40,859 first recorded was a `chapter-004*.md` glob that swept Chapter 40 in from Batch 0004. **The shape judgement stands** — Batch 0005's real improvement is that no chapter in it is two rooms, which was Batch 0004's specific fault and is what cost it its two longest chapters. **The length instruction now lives in `outline/volume-02.md` as lock L22, in the outline, and not only in a state file.**
10. **Threads 1–5** (Holt's posting after Volume 3; the first echo's witness; the abandoned Oren lead in Volume 5; Lira Venn's volume; the teaching question for Bellwright, Keelwright and Chorus Diver). **None is decided here; none is a Volume 01 defect.** Holt's posting is **Volume 03's** decision and Volume 2 must not invent it. The first echo's witness is **open by design** and the volume that first needs him is not Volume 2. The abandoned Oren lead is **Volume 05's** and Volume 2 must not plant one in order to have something to abandon. **Lira Venn's volume is a series-level allocation owed by a series phase, and until it is made no chapter in any volume may use her as a device.** The teaching question is **each ruled by the volume that owns the stage — 5, 9 and 11** — and the rule binding all three is already in force: no convenient master, no teacher who arrives and explains the craft, a team craft and not a gift. The Volume 2 instance is the bellwright and **he teaches nobody.**

**Two mechanical repairs were made in place in this phase and are now on the page.** `chapter-0039.md`: the officer's recalled words of Tuesday the eighth read *at ten feet* and are now **at nine feet**, which is what Chapters 28, 31, 33 and 41 all say. `chapter-0048.md`: the interval to the first of March read *eleven weeks and a day* and is now **eleven weeks and four days**, which is eighty-one days.

**One figure in this file had no chapter behind it and is corrected here and above.** The air total. **Thirteen marks bought since the twenty-eighth of October as at the twenty-eighth of November, which is the figure Chapter 45 reads out in the hearing room, and twenty-nine by the first of December.** The figure of twenty-four, repeated in several places in this file before the audit, is withdrawn.

**Three structural findings are recorded rather than repaired**, per the rule that a structural defect is not repaired silently. **Two chapters that are two rooms:** Chapters 31 and 40 each carry a full hearing agenda end to end, 4,963 and 5,822 words, and the remedy is a shape instruction in the next outline rather than an edit, which is now lock L22 and the batch's shape rules. **Batch 0002 is under the 2,200 floor in five chapters** (967 to 1,062 words) and the remedy is likewise a forward instruction. **The air figure above.** The cheapest honest repair for each is named in the audit.

## Volume 02 — the audited starting state for Chapters 51 onward

**These are the locks a Volume 02 writer inherits, all of them verified against the chapters in this audit and all of them carried into `outline/volume-02.md`.** The outline is the governing document; this section is the quick reference.

1. **Calendar.** 4 January a **Wednesday**, 5 a Thursday, 9 a Monday, 19 a **Thursday**, 1 March a **Wednesday**. **Twenty-six December to the first of March is nine weeks and two days, eighty-one days.** The hearing room's slate wall is wiped every Monday at eight whether there is a sitting or not.
2. **Money.** **Five marks in a bag and nothing in the tin, and nothing has been put into either since the first of December.** Nineteen less three less eleven is five, and the bag has held five since the first of December. The chandler's note took six marks in the three days of the November mole job and none since, is **eleven weeks into six** in the chandler's own book and **thirteen weeks** by the yard's count, and **the chandler has never been asked and neither has Calder** — and Calder said on the first of December that he would take it to him on the second, and the second of December passed. **Forty marks to Rook, sixteen for the reading from the fourteenth of October and twenty-four for the hand from the twenty-ninth, and forty to Venn, first after food, unpaid since the fifteenth of October.** The first four weeks of wages fall in January and February. The freed shoal iron is unsold, worth twenty at a fair sale, a third entered to Sella Brigg **to be paid in February**, and the whole stack is the charter's bond at a clerk's thirteen to the crew.
3. **The delayed note.** **Heard many times, logged eight** — the 26th of October, the 2nd, 4th, 8th, 14th and 23rd of November, the 6th and 26th of December — with the 8th shared with a naval rating's slate. **Two occasions went unwritten: the mole's chain alarm of the 28th of October and the top of the make on the 29th.** The span begins on the **26th of October**, not the 4th of November. The 26th of December's entry has one line more than the others: *knock, delayed, with a call in it. Three marks and a lift. Adult. Not written as a voice.* **The answer inside the note is reserved to the Volume 15 midpoint and Bevin Rook's book is never going to resolve it.**
4. **The concealment, in three parts, closed at the volume's end.** The Oren mark is **in no record, in no office, in no book, and not in Kest's chart notation.** What is on the record is the claim and the refusal to name: a boy's fifteen seconds and no name in the ledger; a slate that says the same; four *not stated* entries and a hand that declined to fill in the fifth on a conservator's certificate of method. **The one false line on the unverified note is left false** — two words would make it true and a true slate with a boy's name in it is a document in a yard any man may read. The three parts: the false line; the boy with the book; the woman with no standing left who knows what is in the anchor and has never been asked to say it.
5. **The ten yards, in one version only.** The withdrawal is **on a wall** — a fresh market-wall sheet in Calder's own hand, *the difference is withdrawn by the yard. See the sheet inside, which says why* — and **what is on no record and on no wall is the difference stated as a measurement.** The first of the three points is not a point: it is a bedded seam of about forty feet in the mole's own foundation, of which the yard used the south end as a position, and the yard has said so itself in print. The tender's count is **nine and ten and a half**; the diver's own is **seven and eleven**, entered as given by Rook on his own statement. **The reeve's ruling that no line may be drawn between the three points stands and is not lifted in Volume 2.**
6. **The compartment.** Spent as a reveal. Clear opening **three foot nine by two foot four**; space **eight foot four by six foot nine clear and eleven feet two deep under the grating**; case **seven to eight feet in its longest face**; **the hatch is the only opening**; the harbor's own thirteen-year-old file says **not cleared, contents not removed**. **Three steps, in that order, whenever the finding is referred to.**
7. **The bell.** Hung **Sunday the 25th of December** on harbor gear in eight and a half feet off the south end, with Kest's chart notation that has never been taken off. **It is not an instrument: it reads nothing, it settles nothing, it says no word.** Fouled waist, tongue half worn through, a year's rope; **a new tongue is a day's work every four or five years for as long as that bell hangs, and the whole of the craft is the going out.**
8. **Custody.** **Three items in the Morrow circuit custody room under three numbers, digits not quoted, and none of them returns.** A supervised look was answered no, or heavily conditional. **The four-hundred-yard bar off the outer marks stands.** The municipal claim on the third item **cannot be withdrawn without a request, and the yard will not make one.** The custody-log re-entry of the twentieth of December gives **no reason**, and **no paper in this harbor or in the circuit office's copying room states what a municipal claim does to a presumed Crown material** — a live instrument, and it may not be answered by a clerk.
9. **Who may appear.** **Ilya Serr as name, seal and office only**, always *Magister Ilya Serr, senior Registry examiner* on a seal line, **never in a room, through Volumes 1–8.** No minister, no ministry naming, no room presence, no Gannet-lie reveal. **Halven Rook** may be named on paper and does not appear in a room. **Captain Holt** may be named, may sign, may send a paper, and is described from a distance only.
10. **The Crown's next move.** A request from the circuit office **worded so that a refusal is a delay**, and **it is the instrument of Mara Venn's Volume 4 break, which may not happen in Volume 2.**
11. **The Volume 2 midpoint.** **A fisher's account of a coast road that no current chart includes, and a claim seal Mara Venn proves is forged.** Not the compartment, and the compartment is not re-used.
12. **Not explained in this volume.** The nine-line mark; the Nine Harbors; the Quietening; the Crown Seal; what was in the sealed case; who cut the aperture.
13. **No faultreader.** **The dressed aperture and the fouling that gives no hour were not seam work and are not seam work.**
14. **Labor stays visible.** The forty marks twice; wages in January and February; Sella Brigg's third in February; the withdrawn cover; the note nobody has re-written; the cracked pump; the *Kittiwake* on the hard; **the harbor's crane and the harbor's barge, which the harbor pays for and the yard does not**; the north berth and the ninth seam out of service; Elias Voke's statement of capacity; the hard at Ashtide. **A poor crew does not get rescued and a charter is not money.**
15. **No confession from Alden Marr.** The partial confession begins in Volume 9; the original repair log is handed over in Volume 15; the plate note closes in Volume 4 with the removed file page.
16. **No formal power stage is taken in Volume 2.** What is paid is the maintenance of one hung bell, by a man with a name, in the weather.
17. **The first of March is the volume's clock.** One herring berth short, the ninth seam's mooring out, the north berth out, nineteen winters of dues disputed and unpaid, and a coast office that answered a letter with the word *capacity*.

---

## Batch 0001 as written (Chapters 51–60), Volume 02 — binding on Chapters 61 onward

**Movement 1, the leash opens, and it does not resolve it.** Everything below is what the ten chapters actually say, in the order they say it, and every figure in it can be checked on the page. Where a chapter and `outline/volume-02.md` disagree, the chapter is right about what happened and the discrepancy is recorded in `state/open-threads.md` rather than reconciled silently.

### 1. The calendar the batch actually ran on

**The volume's own grid is the one the chapters ran on, and `outline/volume-02.md` lock L1 has now been corrected to it.** The prompt's list and the chapters agree: **1 January is a Sunday**, therefore **4 January a Wednesday, 5 a Thursday, 6 a Friday, 7 a Saturday, 9 a Monday, 10 a Tuesday, 11 a Wednesday, 12 a Thursday, 13 a Friday, 14 a Saturday, 15 a Sunday, 16 a Monday, 17 a Tuesday, 18 a Wednesday, 19 a Thursday**, and **1 February and 1 March are both Wednesdays.** Consequences the batch uses: the hearing room's slate wall was last wiped on **Monday the second of January** and is wiped again on **Monday the ninth at eight**, so a figure chalked on the wall on the fourth survives five days and not one; Chapter 51's card figure of a wipe on the fifth is not used, because the fifth is a Thursday; **the first of February is a Wednesday and the boy in Chapter 54 works that out for himself, from the first of March being a Wednesday and a February being four weeks, and checks it a second time off the Mondays, the sixth of February being the Monday after the thirtieth of January**; the 19th of January to the first of March is **forty-one days, five weeks and six days, six weeks less a day**; the 17th to the first of March is **forty-three days, six weeks and a day**; and the 4th of January to the first of March is **exactly eight weeks and both are Wednesdays**. **Twenty-six December to the first of March is sixty-five days, which is nine weeks and two days**; the parenthetical *eighty-one days* was wrong and has now been removed from `outline/volume-02.md`. **The eighth of March is a Wednesday and the tenth is a Friday.**

### 2. The money chain, mark by mark

**Five marks in a bag and nothing in the tin on the fourth of January, and nothing had gone into either since the first of December.** The tin has been empty since the eighteenth of November.

| Date | In | Out | Bag |
|---|---|---|---|
| 4 Jan, Wed | — | — | **5** |
| 5 Jan, Thu | — | 3, the copying-room fee for entering and dating the second request | **2** |
| 6–7 Jan, Fri–Sat | 24 gross, less the harbor's eighth 3, the harbor's share 3, rope and victuals 3 = **15** to the yard; less 1 on the chandler's note | | **16** |
| 11 Jan, Wed | — | 4 hired warp from the *Grey Widow*, 3 the harbor's pump for one tide, 1 oilcloth and candles = **8** | **8** |
