## Volume 03 — the audited starting state for Chapters 101 onward

**These are the locks a Volume 03 writer inherits. `outline/volume-03.md` is the governing document and carries all twenty-eight of them as locks; this is the quick reference, and every figure has been verified in Chapters 51–100 by the audit.**

1. **Calendar.** **The third of May 2026 is a Wednesday and the volume closed on it.** 4 May a Thursday, 8 a Monday, 9 a Tuesday, **15 a Monday**, 16 a Tuesday, 22 a Monday, 23 a Tuesday, 24 a Wednesday; 1 June a Thursday, 1 July a Saturday. **The Mondays in May are the 1st, 8th, 15th, 22nd and 29th; in June the 5th, 12th, 19th and 26th — and the fourth, eleventh and eighteenth of May are Thursdays.** **A wage week falls on a Tuesday: the 2nd, 9th, 16th, 23rd and 30th of May.** **The hearing room's slate wall is wiped every Monday at eight** — the last wipe was Monday the first of May, so in Volume 03 it comes down on the **8th, the 15th, the 22nd and the 29th of May** and on no other day. **The boy is off the stone on Monday the fifteenth of May and no chapter may move it.**
2. **Money.** **Thirteen marks in a bag, twenty-three on the note, nothing in a tin, on the third of May.** The bag stood at nothing for **five days** from the afternoon of the twentieth of April. **The only money this harbor has paid that yard since the first of February is the six marks of the claim window's day rate of the twenty-eighth of April. Other than wages, and other than bread, oil, candles, air, rope, and what has gone onto the note, the only money out of that yard's book to anybody since the first of February is the bellwright's eight marks, out of four hands in a market on the twenty-ninth of April.** **The scoping is binding and was added by a planning-phase review: the unscoped sentence was false on the page, because a wage of four marks a week has gone out of that bag every week since the first of February and sixteen marks of a boy's wage went out of four other men's pockets on the twenty-ninth of April, and `reviews/volume-02/volume-02-close.md` carried the same false sentence and is corrected in place. The point of the sentence is that the bellwright is the only man in this harbor the yard has paid a price of its own to since February.** **A figure written on a note is not a figure paid until somebody puts the money on the counter.** The eighth and the share come off a day rate as well as a raising, and the yard has never argued either.
3. **The chandler's note.** Mark figure **twenty-three**. **Three static counts of its weeks: the creditor's twenty-two, the yard's thirteen, and eleven as the last figure ever written in that page. None has moved since the fourth of January and none may be advanced by arithmetic.** The note is not re-written and the refusal of the thirty-first of March stands unreversed, and the yard said so out loud in a market on the third of May. **A later volume may re-write it or keep it un-written and either is a scene.**
4. **The writ.** A provisional writ of salvage working, granted Monday the first of May, running to the first of March next, **not the Second Bell license, not a charter, renewing nothing, and its ground may not be widened past what it names** — the mole from the first seam to the twelfth, the dump, the north wall, the first and second seams, all ground landward of the third seam, and private ground within the line. **Four names: Calder Marr, Nessa Pike, Bevin Rook, and Tam Slee of the *Grey Widow*, hired and not of this association.** **Only the harbor authority may issue, suspend or renew it, and its own last line says the second season is not its to give.**
5. **The reporting clause.** Four days; every finding of worked glass and every raising of material from **ground which any other party claims — a claim, and a ground closed by a third office's notice is not a claimed ground, and the *Vigil*'s claim lies beyond the outer marks**; place, hour, keeper, limits; a copy to the harbor authority; **the harbor authority may seat one of two disputants and take the finding as given**; production on demand. **Three names on paper and no face in the room.** **It does not reach Mara Venn and she is not of the yard.** **A finding is not a conclusion, and the yard's written objection is in the record.** **The counting rule, binding on every chapter of the volume and stated once in lock L5: the day of a finding is not counted and the return is due on the fourth day after it — a finding of Tuesday the twenty-eighth of April is due Saturday the second of May, which is what the page says. THE RETURNS OF BATCH 0001 ARE ENUMERATED AND ARE TWO: Return 1, Thursday the fourth of May, two days late, about the raising of the twenty-eighth — the iron, the gun-port, and a lump of green glass the yard has not looked at and will not describe; and Return 2, Wednesday the tenth of May, on the day, about the look of the sixth, saying the lump is worth nothing. There is no third, and Tuesday the thirteenth of May carries a dated line in the yard's own book saying no finding was made, nothing was raised, and no return goes up, and that line is not sent to anybody. A day on a mole's crown with the writ in a man's hand and nothing going into the water raises nothing and produces no return. Movement 2 raises worked glass on Saturday the tenth of June, so a THIRD return of the volume falls due on SATURDAY THE SEVENTEENTH OF JUNE, outside Batch 0002.**
6. **The notice of the thirty-first.** Four clauses, not lifted, not narrowed, not re-served, not altered, **and the harbor authority has no power to lift it.** Clause three names three people in the yard who have not been on the authority's work since the day it was served. **It is of the thirty-first of October, and the figure for the whole of Volume 03 is SIX MONTHS — Chapter 100's "eight months" is an error on that page, is not altered, and must not be propagated into any file.** Six acknowledgements in the harbor authority's ledger at the volume's opening, the sixth the second a party asked for; Volume 03's Batch 0001 enters a seventh at the yard's asking in Chapter 102 and an eighth on the reeve's own motion and unasked in Chapter 107, **and there is no ninth: Chapter 109 puts one within reach, asks what it would cost, is told the cost of it, and produces the writ at the top of the mole's steps and not one foot below them, and Chapter 103's day is at the second seam because the seventh is spent. BOTH ACKNOWLEDGEMENTS ARE SPENT AT THE END OF BATCH 0001 AND MOVEMENT 2 ENTERS NONE.** The cost is Kest's and is never free. **A man was inside that closure on the twenty-eighth of April and nearly drowned, and the yard will not say the notice saved him and will not say it was not worth the paper it is on, and both are in one page.**
7. **The plan for the fifth seam.** **Lodged on the seventeenth of January in the yard's own left hand with a fisher's hand on the delivery, and NEVER ANSWERED IN VOLUME 02.** Kest said in his own mouth what would happen to it. **Volume 03 must answer it; the refusal has to cost somebody who is not the yard.** The plate is in eleven feet, seen and not measured and not touched.
8. **The bell.** In eight and a half feet off the south end, fouled, a frame cut through the casting on one side, **no tongue on the frame and two parts of a chain in a lead plug where the tongue was**, a new rope bought on the eleventh of January. **Eight marks and a day of a boat, paid on the twenty-ninth of April, and THE GOING OUT THEY PAID FOR WAS DONE at about ten past three on the second of May in a flat calm, in a boat with Sarn, the holder, Pike and Dace, with the boy on the mole's steps (Ch 99:27), and it is spent.** **The going out recurs in about four years, and the standing sentence said in a street on the third of May stands unreversed: no volume may buy a second going out inside four years, pay a second eight marks, or enter a second going out as done.** It is not an instrument. **The bellwright has been in a boat off the south end for nothing and is owed eight marks and a day of a boat for it, and he is NOT asked his one question in Batch 0001, in a shed, in a boat, in a market or in a room, and the question is carried whole into Movement 2.**
9. **The lead plug.** In a chest in a shed, four descriptions that do not agree, a number in no book. **Not a custody object, not one of the three items, not connected to the ferry plate dug in February, to the Oren mark, to the panel or to the road. The number is written nowhere and the bellwright may be asked in a room and not in a shed — AND BATCH 0001 DOES NOT ASK HIM AT ALL, because the boy who could have read the answer back went off the stone on the fifteenth.**
10. **The glass storm.** Came through the water and not off it, in a second column with a reason, **no cause named in any volume, and it may never become a voice.**
11. **The coast road.** Published in six boats, doubted, half-believed, **not resolved; not explained; not named a network; not connected to anything under the town; the word used once by a rival and refused.** **The nine final routes are not named in the prose. Orison is a place seventy fathoms down, and the yard cannot put that and eleven feet in the same sentence.**
12. **The run under the town.** Four hundred feet of ordinary figures and no chart. **Nine and a quarter feet is the ferry plate in a coaming beyond the eleventh seam and is not one of these figures. Nothing under the town is explained, named, or pursued at night.**
13. **The compartment.** Spent; **not referred to in any chapter of Volume 02**, so the three-step form has not been restated since Chapter 47. **No chapter may say the sealed case was in standing space number four.**
14. **Custody.** Three items, three numbers, digits not quoted, none returns, the municipal claim on the third cannot be withdrawn without a request. **No paper states what a municipal claim does to a presumed Crown material, and that is a live instrument.**
15. **The concealment.** Hidden, told once in Chapter 38, **and not told a second time for nothing.** The false line is still false, the boy has not asked and has not been asked and has entered two omissions in the same breath, and **the woman with no standing has still never been asked to say what is in the anchor, and has said in a room, unprompted, that the writ cannot reach her.**
16. **The delayed note.** **Eleven logged occasions since the twenty-sixth of October, two of them with no call in them, and neither count is advanced by arithmetic or by a measurement.** The delay is a hand's width of a second and has not moved. **The answer inside it is reserved to the Volume 15 midpoint.**
17. **Who may appear.** Ilya Serr on a seal line and never in a room; Halven Rook on paper and not in a room; Captain Holt named, signing, sending a paper, described from a distance, and **not in a room in this volume**; **her posting after her defeat in Volume 03 and the channel by which a disgraced officer reaches a Morrow Quay reeve are Volume 03's own decision.** No new antagonist, no new institution, no new final enemy.
18. **Labor.** The forty marks twice; the wage with the fifth of March the oldest line; the eight marks to the bellwright **paid**; the eight shillings and tenpence and four hundred feet of warp on the bottom; the note at twenty-three and not re-written; no gland, no hose, no air; the *Kittiwake* on the hard; the harbor's gear on its own account; the north berth out; the ninth seam out with no end of it entered; the tide gang four short; the cistern on a rota; the dead hose; the light not on; **the harbor authority with no boat of its own**; the *Sea Wren* at the pile; the hard at Ashtide; nineteen winters; **the eleven copies and the four unreached holders not asked**; and **Neave Tarrant, nine winters, not found.**
19. **Power.** **No stage is taken in Volume 02. The Faultreader stage belongs to Volume 03, late and badly, and a seam identifies difference and not truth.** The condition was named once in Volume 02, in Chapter 88, and not used again in it. **Volume 03 may name it once and no more, and lock L25 settles which movement spends it: Movement 1 is the movement that may name it; if it does, no chapter in Movements 2 to 5 may name it again; if it does not, the one naming is available to one later movement and to no more than one, and Movement 4 is where the volume's structure wants it.**
20. **The shape rule.** **"No chapter is two rooms" is WITHDRAWN. No cost is paid twice, and every date in a chapter belongs to one day.** A rescue and a hearing are split across two days, a hearing room and a market are split across two days, **and the hearing room and the market wall are never the same day.** Ten speeches are not ten chapters. **A single-day multi-room chapter is ALLOWED and is not a fault, and Chapters 92, 98 and 100 are the precedent and may not be re-raised against: each is one day in two or three rooms. A man may be in three rooms in one day; a man may not be paid for two days in one chapter; no day may be in a chapter twice.**
21. **The pump.** Housing fitted on the eighth of March. Hired and a half day by Harl Dace on Tuesday the twenty-first of March (Ch 78). **THE INHERITED FIGURE IS NOT OUT OF THE SHED SINCE WEDNESDAY THE FIFTH OF APRIL, when Iven Toft hired it for the day at four marks (Ch 82) — not "since the housing came up" and not "since March".** A hire was asked for on Saturday the twenty-second of April by a man with money and missed, the pump being on the mole and the yard in a coal store, **and the yard entered that refusal against itself with no reason against it (Ch 92).** Chapter 100's read-back of "not out of this shed since the twenty-first of March" was on the thirty-first of March and was true that day. **Chapter 104 has the same man back and gets the pump: two days at four marks a day, eight marks in, two days lost, and he says he would have paid four and a quarter.**
22. **The wage column.** Four lines and sixteen marks with the fifth of March the oldest, as read out on Wednesday the third of May. **THE THIRTEENTH WEEK, four marks, FELL DUE ON TUESDAY THE SECOND OF MAY AND IS IN NO COLUMN — a Volume 02 omission, closed in Chapter 101, where the boy enters it himself on the fourth with the reason that it went in on the fourth, and the column becomes five lines and twenty marks.** A wage week falls on a Tuesday and is paid or entered unpaid on that Tuesday.
23. **The iron of the twenty-eighth of April.** **Four hundred and forty pounds of old iron in three pieces, one of them a bell-metal gun-port off something with a gun in it, raised off the mole's own face between the third and fourth seams under a claim window that has been spent and logged, none of it glass and all of it heavy (Ch 94:67). IT HAD NO CLOSING POSITION IN VOLUME 02 AND IS NOW GIVEN ONE: unsold, on the yard's own ground behind the shed, in the same heap as the twenty marks of the yard's own iron and a saw kerf in a crown shackle, in a heap the yard may not sell because it has declared a thing about its own gear in a book and a buyer must read the declaration first.** The gun-port is the one piece of the three that is not iron, **and the only man in this harbor who would know it at a glance is the harbor's bellwright, who is not of this yard, is not of the Free Keel, teaches nobody, and is not asked about it — the number in the lead plug is the only question this volume has for him.** The nineteen marks of iron on the page in March was the pump's housing and is a different sum for a different casting and the two are never put in the same sentence.

---

## Batch 0001 as written (Chapters 101–110), Volume 03 — binding on Chapter 111 onward

**Movement 1, the first return and the first bill. THE FOUR-DAY CLOCK RUNS TWICE, THE BOY GOES OFF THE STONE, THE RIVAL IS REFUSED A DAY, THE PLAN OF THE SEVENTEENTH OF JANUARY IS REFUSED, THE WRIT IS PRODUCED ON A MOLE, AND ONE BOAT GOES OFF THE SOUTH END AND IS NOT A GOING OUT.** The chapters are the authority on what happened. Everything in this section is taken from Chapters 101–110 and from nothing else, and the whole batch is **26,767 words** (Ch101 2,341 · Ch102 2,562 · Ch103 2,562 · Ch104 2,627 · Ch105 2,398 · Ch106 2,531 · Ch107 2,759 · Ch108 2,583 · Ch109 3,215 · Ch110 3,189).

### 1. The calendar the batch actually ran on

**`outline/volume-03.md` lock L1's grid, unchanged, and the chapters ran on it without an exception.** The third of May a Wednesday, and therefore 4 a **Thursday**, 5 a Friday, 6 a **Saturday**, 7 a Sunday, 8 a **Monday**, 9 a **Tuesday**, 10 a **Wednesday**, 11 a Thursday, 12 a **Friday**, 13 a **Saturday**, 14 a Sunday, **15 a Monday**, 16 a Tuesday, 17 a **Wednesday**, 18 a **Thursday**, 19 a Friday, 20 a **Saturday**, 21 a Sunday, 22 a **Monday**, 23 a Tuesday, 24 a **Wednesday**. **The Mondays in May are the 1st, 8th, 15th, 22nd and 29th, and the first of May is the first Monday of May; the fourth, eleventh and eighteenth of May are Thursdays.** **The wage Tuesdays in this batch are the ninth and the sixteenth, and the sixteenth is the first wage week in this column's history that has gone by without a line in it, and the twenty-third is the second.**

**The ten days of story are the fourth to the twenty-fourth of May, and every chapter carries its own dates:** Ch101 the 4th and 5th, Ch102 the 6th and 7th, Ch103 the 8th and 9th, Ch104 the 10th and 11th, Ch105 the 12th and 13th, Ch106 the 14th and 15th, Ch107 the 16th and 17th, Ch108 the 18th and 19th, Ch109 the 20th, 21st and 22nd, Ch110 the 23rd and 24th. **No day is in a chapter twice and no man is paid for two days in one chapter.**

**The hearing room's slate wall.** It is wiped every Monday at eight whether there is a sitting or not. **The last wipe before this batch was Monday the first of May and the one chalk figure went off it on Monday the eighth at eight; the wall was bare at eight on the eighth, on the fifteenth and on the twenty-second, and there was no sitting on any of the three days and nothing was put up in its place on any of them.** The clerk kept his own page on all three mornings and said so each time: a side and three-quarters on the eighth, a side and a half on the fifteenth, and on the twenty-second that the first time in five months he has come up the hill and been given less than he came for. **The hearing room itself is used once in this batch, on Wednesday the seventeenth, for the eighth of the reeve's acknowledgements; and the market wall is used once, on Thursday the eighteenth. No two institutional days in this batch are the same day and neither is a Monday.** Chapter 101 mentions the wall's one chalk figure and is at the fish dock and the mole's steps, and Chapter 104's figures are read back at the head of the dock and not on a wall.

**The age of the notice of the thirty-first of October, in the book's own arithmetic: six months and two days on the third of May, six months and three days on the fourth, six months and five days on the sixth, six months and a week on the eighth, and six months and sixteen days on the seventeenth.** The figure **eight months is a Chapter 100 error, is not repeated anywhere in Chapters 101–110, and is not to be propagated into any later file.**

### 2. The money chain, mark by mark

**Twenty-seven marks in a bag, thirty-one on the chandler's note, and nothing in a tin, on Wednesday the twenty-fourth of May.**

| Date | In | Out | Bag | Note |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Wed 3 May | — | — | **13** | 23 |
| Thu 4 May | — | nothing moves. **Return 1 made, two days late.** The thirteenth week of a wage, four marks, entered by the boy in his own right hand, Tuesday the second of May, unpaid, with the reason that it went in on the fourth — **the column becomes five lines and twenty marks** | **13** | 23 |
| Fri 5 – Sun 7 May | — | — | **13** | 23 |
| Mon 8 May | **26 in**, gross of a day at the second seam on the harbor authority's own account at the yard's own rate, counted out of the clerk's coat with the hour written and read back twice — the eighth three, the share three, a pot of the office's air two, rope one, the victuals two, a hand three, and Wil Slee of the tide gang on the office's own hand and not in the column | **the fourteenth week of a wage is not paid today** | **39** | 23 |
| Tue 9 May | — | **4**, the fourteenth week of a wage, paid on the Tuesday out of the day, and not announced on the Monday | **35** | 23 |
| Wed 10 May | **4**, the pump hired out for the day at four marks | **3**, a fortnight of bread, in the creditor's own hand. **The gland goes on the page at the creditor's own figure of five in the creditor's own hand, on a page standing at twenty-three, and the carting is not on the page**, which takes the page to twenty-eight and then to thirty-one | **36** | **31** |
| Thu 11 May | **4**, the second day of the same pump | **1**, a man and a handcart two miles up the coast road and back with the casting | **39** | 31 |
| Fri 12 – Sun 14 May | — | — | **39** | 31 |
| Mon 15 May | — | **4**, the fifteenth week of a wage, paid on the Monday and not the Tuesday for a reason entered on the page | **35** | 31 |
| Tue 16 – Mon 22 May | — | nothing moves. **No wage week is entered on the sixteenth or the twenty-third, and the reason is on the page: this yard has no reader** | **35** | 31 |
| Tue 23 May | — | **8**, a boat for the day and a man to row her, out of the pump's eight marks | **27** | 31 |
| Wed 24 May | — | nothing moves, and the yard says out loud that it is short and is not pretending it is not | **27** | 31 |

**The full chain: 13, 13, 13, 39, 35, 36, 39, 39, 35, 35, 27, 27** in the bag, and **23, 23, 23, 23, 23, 31, 31** on the note. The tin is empty and has been since the twenty-eighth of April.**

**Three things about this chain that a later phase may not get wrong.**
1. **The bag moves for the first time since the twenty-ninth of April and it moves twice, and both movements are the yard's own decision.** The twenty-six of Monday the eighth is the only money this harbor has paid that yard since the first of February, and it is the second time it has done so; the first was six marks on the twenty-eighth of April for air and a pump. **The only money this harbor has paid that yard since the first of February is now twenty-six plus six, and the yard says both figures out loud and does not pretend the second is the first.**
2. **The eight marks of the pump is the whole of the money Chapter 110 has for a boat's day, and the chapter says where it came from, on the page, before anybody gets wet.** The pump is not out of this shed again inside four years without a reason of that kind.
3. **The three counts of the note's weeks — the creditor's twenty-two, the yard's thirteen, and eleven as the last figure ever written in that page — do not move, and were not moved, and are not moved by the gland or by the bread.** The note is not re-written. The refusal of the thirty-first of March stands unreversed.

**Debts, all standing, all unpaid, none reduced, except the one that is paid and the one that is new.**

- **PAID — eight marks and a day of a boat to the harbor's bellwright, Corvin Sarn**, on Saturday the twenty-ninth of April, out of four hands in a market and not one mark out of the yard's own work. Unchanged in this batch. **What is new is that the going out it bought was done at about ten past three on Tuesday the second of May in a flat calm and is spent, and the next one is in about four years, and the standing sentence said in a street on the third of May stands unreversed.**
- **NEW, AND OWED: eight marks and a day of a boat, entered as owing to the same man a second time inside six weeks, entered as owing and not as paid.** It is entered on Tuesday the twenty-third of May, in the yard's own hand, with the sentence that a going out is bought and named and paid and entered and this was a boat hired for a day, that the yard has no instrument for that man, did not buy one, and is not going to, and that it is not going to be thanked for not asking. **It is the batch's standing owing and it is a craft that recurs.**
- **A wage at four marks a week from the first of February.** The column closes the batch **five lines and twenty marks, the fifth of March the oldest**, and the fifth stays in it on purpose. The fourteenth week is paid out of the day rate on the ninth; the fifteenth is paid on the Monday the fifteenth in advance, with the reason that this yard is not going to pay a boy a wage on a Tuesday with a hand that cannot hold a pencil in it; **the sixteenth falls on Tuesday the sixteenth of May and no line goes in for it, and the reason that goes in is that this yard has no reader, and the twenty-third is the same.** **Movement 2 inherits a wage that runs on and a column nobody is filling, and the two have to be kept apart.**
- **Forty marks to Bevin Rook** — sixteen for the reading of the book from the fourteenth of October, twenty-four for the hand from the twenty-ninth — **stand whole, unpaid, not reduced, not asked to be reduced, and not one mark paid in this batch, and nobody in that crew asked him one word about either of the two omissions he entered, then or afterwards.**
- **Forty marks to Mara Venn**, first after food, unpaid since the fifteenth of October, **not paid and not paid out of any market money and not reachable by the writ.** She is in a market on the eighteenth of May, not asked, prices six marks and two days out loud, repeats that a shop at Ashtide refused her four marks in writing for an opinion she gave in a market, and says the forty is not one mark smaller for it. She is not of the yard, is not an assistant, and has not been asked to be useful.
- **Eight shillings and tenpence to Sella Brigg**, undiminished and unpaid, with about four hundred feet of her own warp on the bottom of this harbor.
- **Twenty marks of unsold iron with a saw kerf in one crown shackle, and four hundred and forty pounds of old iron in three pieces off the mole's own face between the third and fourth seams raised on the twenty-eighth of April, in the same heap behind the shed, one of the three a bell-metal gun-port.** Unsold, unweighed, unpriced, not offered to anybody, and **in a heap the yard may not sell one mark's worth of without a buyer reading the declaration first.** The nineteen marks of iron on the page on the eighth of March was the pump's housing and is a different sum for a different casting in a different month, and the two are never in the same sentence.

**What the air cost and what it bought. A compressor's gland is bought, at the creditor's own figure of five marks, on a page standing at twenty-three, and the carting is not on the page. There is still no delivery hose in this harbor and there is no air in this harbor.** The one pot of air in this batch was bought of the harbor authority at the yard's own two-mark rate, filled at the office's compressor and carried on the office's own hose, used on the eighth of May, and it was the whole of the air in this harbor both before and after, and it is an empty pot on a cart. **The gland is not fitted and will not be for nine days and the pump is on a lighterman's wharf until the Friday morning, and the yard has entered that it is going to be without air for nine days and has said so out loud twice.**

### 3. Every document and instrument created in this batch

- **Return 1, Thursday the fourth of May, two days late**, one page, in Calder Marr's own left hand, read out by the boy and then by the clerk and read out twice. It states the finding of Tuesday the twenty-eighth of April, the mole's own face between the third and fourth seams, under a claim window of one date, with two men not of the party in the water each fixing his own limit at the boat; **what was raised, being four hundred and forty pounds of old iron in three pieces of which the second is a bell-metal gun-port off something that had a gun in it, the first piece — a chain shackle and a turn of old cable — up at about twenty-six minutes past eleven in the morning, and the three pieces off the bedded material at about twenty past one in the afternoon; and a lump of green glass in a bucket.** Place, hour, keeper and limits, with **the keeper's line disclosing that the object was in a boat in a shed in this harbor, which the yard entered of itself and unasked.** The two days between the finding and the return are on the page and not in a margin. **The yard's own position is entered as a fact and not as an argument: the party does not know which limb of the clause caught the lump and has not waited to find out, and a lump of green glass is itself a finding of worked glass on the twenty-eighth of April, so the first limb is enough.** A copy went to the harbor authority's ledger in the clerk's own hand.
- **The clerk's own line of the fourth of May, his own book, not the yard's and not the office's record of the return:** that the object was in a shed of this harbor from the twenty-eighth of April to that day, that the party entered the fact itself in its own return without being asked, and that the office has had a notice on the mole since the thirty-first of October which says it may not be in there, and has not argued the notice. **That is the second half of a figure and the clerk said so.**
- **The second column's line about the six days**, entered in Calder Marr's own left hand on the fourth: the reason is that no man on a mole in this harbor could check where a bucket was standing between the twenty-eighth of April and the fourth of May, and that is true, and it is also the reason a man in a city is going to be able to check it, and both are in the same line.
- **The thirteenth week of a wage**, entered by the boy in his own right hand on the fourth of May: four marks, Tuesday the second of May, unpaid, with the reason that it went in on the fourth.
- **The seventh of the reeve's acknowledgements**, Saturday the sixth of May, in Kest's own hand, read out on the crown and read back by the clerk, at the yard's asking and the third time in three months, for the party standing on the mole's crown between the third and ninth seams in the open air with a bucket, going in no water and raising nothing. It names the six before it, says the sixth of them was the twenty-seventh of April and was asked for by a party, says the cost of all of them is the reeve's and is never free, and says the office has no power to lift the notice and is not lifting it and has never lifted it.
- **The two columns of the lump of green glass**, Saturday the sixth, read back by the boy and by the other three. First column: the object, the sun edge, **the scalloped edge of eleven scallops in about four inches which is not a fracture and not a mould line**, the broken face, the place it came out of, that it carries no claim seal and is in no office's book and has no number anywhere, that it is not a panel, and that it is not the ferry plate and not the worked panel given back in a market on the seventh of April and is not connected with it. Second column, with a reason against every line: the mass of it, the sound of it struck once on a board with a wrench handle, and the look of it. **And the age of it goes in neither column**, on a line of its own, with the reason that the party declines to give a figure for it and declines to give a reason for declining.
- **The finding of the sixth of May and the date of the second return, said out loud on the crown** — the sixth is the finding, the day of the finding is not counted, the return is due on the tenth — **and the clerk entered the finding and the day in the first column because it was a statement of fact made by a man who knows the trade and could be asked about it again in a market on Friday.**
- **The harbor authority's day order of the eighth of May**, its own paper, for a day at the second seam on the office's own account at the yard's own rate under the writ, out of the harbor's own day work and not out of the repair account, which has been empty since the middle of February.
- **The reason of Monday the eighth of May**, in Calder Marr's own left hand, in the harbor authority's own book, with the clerk present, unedited and unsummarized: the ground landward of the third seam's mark and nothing above it and nothing under the bedded material; **the purpose, being a ring and a shackle of the office's own ground tackle and about four feet of herring warp that is a herring boat's own, on her own account, and is not being claimed**; the two divers, **Tam Slee of the *Grey Widow* — the fourth person on the writ — and Wil Slee of this harbor's own tide gang**, neither of them of the party, each fixing his own limit at the boat; **the tender as Neale, foreman of the tide gang, not a diver, the cutoff his to pull without asking anybody**; the air as the office's own; **and the sentence that the party is not on the office's work and is standing on that ground as the owner of a bucket and of nothing else.** The eighth and the share come off the day rate, with Nessa Pike's name against the argument for taking them and the name of the man who ordered it against her name.
- **Return 2, Wednesday the tenth of May, on the day it was due**, one page, shorter than the first, in the yard's own left hand, read by the boy and by the clerk and read twice: the finding of the sixth at about eleven in the morning on the crown in the open air in daylight; the place, the hour, **the keeper and the fact that the object is on the mole's own ground in the open air under a tarpaulin and is not in any shed and is not in any boat**; the limits, **that the object is worth nothing to anybody the party has been able to find out about**, that it is not a panel and is not the ferry plate and is not the worked panel of the seventh of April, that its age is not figured and no reason for declining is given, and that **the scalloped edge is in the first column because a man on a mole can look at an edge, and the party's sentence is that it does not know what the edge means and a clause asks for a finding and not a meaning.** The clerk wrote on his own page that the first of the two was two days late and the second was on the day and both are the same instrument.
- **The gland on the chandler's page**, Wednesday the tenth, the creditor's own figure of five marks in the creditor's own hand on a page standing at twenty-three, **the carting not on the page**, and a fortnight of bread at three the same afternoon.
- **The pump's hire**, Wednesday the tenth and Thursday the eleventh, two days at four marks a day, eight marks in, the counter read out as four in silver and four in small coin, the refusal of the twenty-second of April entered against the yard with no reason against it, **and the whole of the hirer saying the man in his own words, including that he would have paid four and a quarter and should not have been made to come up the hill twice for it.** Nessa Pike's signature against losing the pump.
- **The refused day**, Friday the twelfth. **Tarner is entered in the yard's book as the fifth man refused on that mole since the fourteenth of October, in the yard's own words and with his name on it**, and the reason is entered: a man who is not on the writ is a man who cannot be reported.
- **The copy of the tenth thing** given to Noll Tarner in his own hand at no charge with his name on it, **with the count of the six hand-copies expressly not altered and the reason entered, because this is a copy of a copy and no two men in this harbor are to be able to tell in four years which is which.** Tarner's own statement that he is not going to sell it and not going to give it to a naval officer goes in the first column.
- **The line of Saturday the thirteenth of May**, in the yard's own book, **not sent to anybody and not in the harbor authority's ledger and not in a circuit office**: no finding of worked glass and nothing raised off ground any other party claims since the sixth, no return goes up, and the reason, and that a return about nothing is not a smaller return but is not a return. Read back by the boy and by the other two.
- **The refusal of the eight-clause plan**, Tuesday the sixteenth of May, four pages, signed on the twelfth of January and served on the sixteenth, on four grounds, every one of them right, with Elias Voke in the room because he carried it on the seventeenth of January.
- **The eighth of the reeve's acknowledgements**, Wednesday the seventeenth of May, in Kest's own hand, unasked by any party, and the **ninth of his instruments and the second of his claim windows in a month:** Monday the twelfth of June, at the top of the flood, on the mole's toe and inner face between the third and ninth seams, the officer of the office with the clerk and two tide-gang men, to enter in the office's own book what the notice of the thirty-first has cost this harbor authority; **no person of the yard upon that ground and not to be asked for and would be refused**; the notice not lifted, not narrowed, not re-served, not altered; the seventh named, and the six before it, and the sentence that the sixth and the seventh were each asked for by a party and this eighth was asked for by nobody, and the cost is the reeve's and is never free.
- **The four-sentence answer on the back of a slate**, Thursday the eighteenth, in Calder Marr's own hand, read aloud in a market and copied onto another man's slate, and entered in the yard's own book in the second column with a reason.
- **The demand of Saturday the twentieth of May**, four lines, in Ambrose Sell's hand, naming Monday the twenty-second at ten at the top of the mole's steps landward of the third seam's mark, and stating that it is a demand and not a complaint and that no answer is asked for.
- **The answer given to the clerk on the Saturday, out loud, in front of two men, and written down by him:** that the yard will stand on the top of the steps landward of the third seam's mark, where the ground the notice of the thirty-first closes begins below. **And a second line under it in the clerk's own book, his own reading and not a ruling**, that the ground the notice closes begins below the top of the mole's steps at Morrow Quay, and that this office has no power to narrow that notice and has not attempted to narrow it this morning.
- **The third column's Sunday line**, the twenty-first of May, in Calder Marr's own left hand, under the two times: that no man in the party is filling it, that no man in the party can now say whether the two times are the same time, and that the party is going to keep the column and is not going to scratch out the lines in it and is not going to pretend it is current, **and the reason is that a column with a head on it and nothing under it is the honest shape of this yard on the twenty-first of May and a man in a city is owed that shape and not a better one.**
- **The production of Monday the twenty-second**, on a mole, in the open air: the writ with the clause and the three names on the clause and no face in the room; the crew's book, entire, both columns and the third column and the reasons; **both returns, unshortened**; the tenth thing; the slip with the eleven names and the four unreached holders named. **And the sentence that there is no third return and no fourth, that this yard has made two, and that if a third is asked for this yard will say it has made two.**
- **The difference between a going out and a boat**, entered in the yard's own left hand on the morning of the twenty-third, before anybody got wet, and the standing sentence of the third of May set under it unreversed.
- **The eight marks and a day of a boat entered as owing to the harbor's bellwright a second time**, on the twenty-third, in the yard's own left hand, and not as paid.
- **The two columns of the twenty-third**, carrying only the part of the bellwright's account that is new, and the same three lines of the second column with the same reasons, because a man is not to be asked to say the same thing twice.

**There is no ninth acknowledgement, there is no fifth seam access, and there is no day in this batch on which a person, a boat or a lead of this yard went inside the first clause of the notice of the thirty-first of October except the sixth of May, and that was a person with a bucket in the open air on a crown and no water and no raising, entered as the seventh.**

### 4. The bodies and the crew

- **Calder Marr**, 32, line diver with an unstable tide-ear capacity used twice and not used since the eighth of December, and the lift at the end of a childhood call gone and not restorable. **He goes in no water in this batch, enters nothing, and takes no stage of any kind.** **THE CONDITION IS NOT NAMED IN THIS BATCH.** It appears twice as the bare fact — on the mole's steps on the fourth, in the form of a man who cannot place a sound being given a sentence; and in the boat off the south end on the twenty-third, where the tide bell goes at two o'clock, the water gives it back a hand's width of a second later, there is no boy on the steps with a slate and a stopwatch, and he cannot say in that boat which of the two sounds is in the boat — and both times it goes into the second column with a reason against it and nobody in that crew asks him about it. **On the twenty-third a man of fifty gives him a sentence for it and Nessa Pike puts it in his own words and says nobody is going to ask him about it and that there is nobody left in this yard who would think of it. The naming is NOT spent by this batch, and Movement 4 of this volume is where the volume's own structure wants it spent.**
- **Nessa Pike**, 27. **She is right in this batch four times and every one of them costs something.** She insists the lump be entered as worked glass in the first column and the yard's "it is nothing" in the first column and not the second; she signs her own name against taking the eighth and the share on the authority's account and puts the name of the man who ordered it against hers; she signs against losing the pump for two days; she fixes the air paragraph; and on the mole's steps on the twenty-second she tells a clerk of a circuit office in advance that she will be four feet from the holder of the writ for the whole of it. **Nobody thanks her for any of it and she does not want to be thanked.**
- **Bevin Rook**, 22, **off the stone on Monday the fifteenth of May and not replaced.** His hand had been going since the fourth of April and the yard said so in a room on the first of May. He is the reader for two hundred days; he reads the position out one last time and reads it twice, the second time in eleven minutes against seven; he says the thing nobody asked for twice more, in the middle of a list and not at the end of it, on the eighth and the tenth; he is paid four marks for the week of the ninth to the fifteenth, on the Monday; and **he leaves the book on the bench with the pencil on top of it and says the sentence that is the batch's centre.** Nobody in that crew asks him one word about either of the two omissions he entered, then or afterwards. The forty marks of claim stand whole.
- **Corvin Sarn**, 50, the harbor's own bellwright, **paid**, teaching nobody, of no crew of this association. **He is NOT asked the one question in this batch — not in a shed, not in a boat, not in a market, not on a mole, not in a room — and the yard has the question ready and says the reason out loud on the water, and the reason is the boy's hand and not the man's manner.** He comes out in a boat he is not paid to be in, gives the part of his account that is new and only that, does not look away, is not thanked, says the delay has never moved in his time in the harbor and will not stand in a boat and say why, puts a hand on a line and says one word, and says the thing he said in a shed doorway in April and is told it went on a wall and the light is not on.
- **Noll Tarner**, 34, of the *Sea Wren*, at the pile since the fourth of March. He asks for a day, **is refused in a form he can check, is given a free copy of the tenth thing with his name on it, and does not become a villain and does not ask twice.** He supplies the turn — *a road that no chart carries is a thing a man can sell, and the first question is not what is under it, it is who pays for the map* — and **names Lowfen**, about nine miles off, where the channels are kept by hand by men who do not want a diver, which goes in the second column with a reason because he has not been there. **He is never thanked.**
- **Elias Voke**, 51, of the *Anna Ray*, nineteen winters disputed and unpaid. **The price of a correct refusal lands on him, in a room, in front of the man who wrote it**, and he does not shout. He asks for his name and his boat and the nineteen winters in his own hand in the account the harbor authority is sending up the coast, and says one sentence that is his and not the reeve's.
- **Mara Venn**, 30, no office, a trade, **expensive, right, and not broken.** She is in a market on the eighteenth of May, not asked, **prices six marks and two days out loud**, says this yard has had two of her findings for nothing and has not been told what they cost, repeats the refused four marks in writing from a shop at Ashtide, and says the forty marks is not one mark smaller for it. **She is not asked to be useful, she is not of the yard, she is not an assistant, and the writ cannot reach her. Her break is Volume 4 and nothing in this batch is a step toward it.**
- **Harl Dace**, 48 — the herring warp of the eighth of May goes back onto the third lay's wharf in his own hands and is not claimed by anybody. **Tarner's man of fifty-one with a bad wheel is not in this batch, is not on any lead, and is not asked anything.**
- **Wil Slee and Tam Slee**, the two men in the water on the eighth of May. **There is one man of the *Grey Widow* and he is Tam Slee, who is the fourth name on the writ; the other is Wil Slee of this harbor's own tide gang; and Neale, Wil Slee's brother-in-law, is the tender. There are not two men of the *Grey Widow* in this batch and a chapter that says so has stopped reading.**
- **Neale**, foreman of the harbor's tide gang, is the tender on the eighth of May with the cutoff his, and holds the lead line for twenty-two minutes. He is a harbor-authority man and is not a diver and is not of the party, and the reason says why he has to be.
- **The boatman of the south lay** who hires the boat and rows it on the twenty-third: unnamed, of the south lay, nineteen years, who has charged eleven a day since he was twenty-four, who says the eight is below the going rate out loud so that the yard knows what it has got, and who is in the first column because a man in a shed can be asked.
- **The man with a slate**, Thursday the eighteenth: unnamed, trades up and down this coast, is nobody's clerk, was in the street at the head of this yard's own dock on the third of May with the same slate, has been asking the same four questions since the middle of April, and has written down every answer he has been given including the noes. **He is the first customer the coast road has had and he is not the Crown and not an office, and the yard refuses to sell him a copy, and his four pages are in a market in this harbor and are not the yard's to correct.**
- **The chandler at the sign of the Drowned Bell**, unnamed as on the page, who entered five marks in his own hand and would not put the carting on it and said why without being asked.
- **The harbor authority's clerk and Ambrose Sell, a clerk of the circuit office**, are clerks, and say what a box can hold, and **neither is an antagonist and neither may become one.** Sell writes down where the yard will stand and why, and writes a second line under it in his own hand that he may be wrong about.
- **Alden Marr** is at the far bench in Chapters 101, 104, 106 and 110, is not asked for anything, and **Calder Marr does not go over to him. No fragment, no report talk, no second story, no confession.**
- **The woman in the net loft** who said on the second of May that the hand lead's line would be right in a fortnight or not at all says on the twenty-fourth, in about nine words, that it is right, and that she took eleven days instead of seven because a boy went off a stone that week.

### 5. The dated positions at the end of Chapter 110

- **The writ.** A provisional writ of salvage working granted Monday the first of May, running to the first of March next, **not the Second Bell license, not a charter, renewing nothing, and its ground not widened**: the mole from the first seam to the twelfth, the dump, the north wall, the first and second seams, all ground landward of the third seam, and private ground within the line. **The four names are unchanged and no fifth name has been added anywhere**: Calder Marr, Nessa Pike, Bevin Rook, and Tam Slee of the *Grey Widow*, hired and not of this association. **It was produced on demand on Monday the twenty-second of May at the top of the mole's steps, in the open air, above the third seam's mark, with the clause, the book, both returns and two public sheets, and nothing else.** The second season is a named absence and no chapter has manufactured a renewal.
- **The reporting clause.** **Two returns have been made and there is no third and no fourth.** Return 1 on the fourth of May, two days late. Return 2 on the tenth of May, on the day. A dated line in the yard's own book on the thirteenth of May says no return goes up and why, and that line has not been sent to anybody. **A third return of the volume falls due on Saturday the seventeenth of June, out of a shard of worked glass raised on Saturday the tenth of June on ground landward of the third seam, in a boat on a mole because the second clause keeps it out of every shed — and it is Movement 3 that makes it, and Movement 2 does not.** **The second limb is a claim and not a closure, and a ground the notice closes is not a claimed ground, and the *Vigil's* claim lies beyond the outer marks and does not touch the mole's face: the yard relies on the first limb in Return 1 on the ground that a lump of green glass is itself a finding of worked glass, and says it does not know which limb caught it and has not waited to find out.**
- **The notice of the thirty-first of October.** Four clauses, not lifted, not narrowed, not re-served, not altered, **and the harbor authority has no power to lift it and has said so in its own ledger for the eighth time and did not lift it on the seventeenth of May either.** Clause two is on the page three times as a reason and is not argued by anybody: the lump was not looked at in a shed, was looked at on a crown in daylight, and is now on a crown under a tarpaulin and two stones. Clause three names Calder Marr, Nessa Pike and Bevin Rook and none of the three has been on the authority's work, which is why the tender on the eighth of May had to be Neale. **There are eight acknowledgements in the harbor authority's ledger at the end of this batch and both this batch's are spent, so MOVEMENT 2 ENTERS NONE AND PUTS NO PERSON, BOAT OR LEAD OF THIS YARD INSIDE THE FIRST CLAUSE.** The cost of all eight is the reeve's and is never free.
- **The eighth-clause plan for the fifth seam is REFUSED and the refusal is in a record with a date on it.** Four grounds, all right. The plate is still in eleven feet in the mole's own foundation stone, **seen and not measured and not touched, and nobody in this harbor has said one word about it since the seventeenth of May.** What would move it is a change of politics in an office on a schedule nobody can name, and the first act of that change is a claim window for Monday the twelfth of June, in the office's own account, to go out and write down what the notice has cost — **and it is not for this yard, this yard is not to be on that ground, and no person of this yard is to be asked to be.**
- **The bell.** Eight and a half feet off the south end, fouled, a frame cut through the casting on one side, **no tongue on the frame at all and two parts of a chain in a lead plug where the tongue was**, a new rope bought on the eleventh of January. **The going out of the second of May is done and is spent, and it may not be done again inside four years, and no second eight marks is paid, and the standing sentence said in a street on the third of May stands unreversed.** **What this batch adds is a boat that is not a going out, on the yard's own account, out of the pump's eight marks, with the harbor's own bellwright in it for nothing, and eight marks and a day of a boat entered as owing to him a second time in six weeks.** The delay is a hand's width of a second and has not moved, and it is not entered as an occasion, and the count of logged occasions stands at eleven and the two empty answers at two.
- **The lead plug.** Untouched. In a chest in a shed, four descriptions that do not agree, a number in no book, the boy having written on the lid that there is nothing in it that anybody in this harbor can check. **The bellwright was not asked the one question, and the yard says on the water that the reason is the boy's hand and not the man's manner.**
- **The glass storm.** Untouched and not used. It is in the second column with a reason against it and **no cause has been named for it and none may be, in this or any later volume, and it may never become a voice.**
- **The coast road.** Published in six boats in six hands, **and it has its first customer and he is nobody's clerk, and the yard refuses to sell him a copy, and the reason is given on the boards and copied into his own hand.** The word is not on the wall. **No document has come back out of a city in this batch.** The road is not explained, not named, not connected to anything under the town, and the two are never fused. The nine final routes are not named in the prose. Orison is a place seventy fathoms down and the yard cannot put that and eleven feet in the same sentence.
- **Custody.** Three items, three numbers, digits not quoted, none returns, none is asked for, the municipal claim on the third stands and the yard will not make a request. **No paper states what a municipal claim does to a presumed Crown material, and no clerk answered it in this batch and none may in any volume.**
- **The concealment.** Hidden. The Oren mark is in no record, in no office, in no book and not in Kest's chart notation. The false line on the unverified slate is still false. **Nobody in this batch asked the boy, and nobody asked the conservator, and the boy was asked one word about neither of his two omissions.** It was told once, in Chapter 38, and it is not told again.
- **The three custody numbers, the compartment, the ten yards, the run under the town, the first echo's witness, the nine-line mark, the dressed aperture, the plate dug on the mole's toe in February, and the ferry plate.** All untouched by this batch. No chapter of Chapters 101–110 refers to the compartment, and the three-step finding is therefore not restated. No line is drawn between the three points and the reeve's ruling stands. **Nine and a quarter feet is not mentioned anywhere in this batch.**
- **Labor.** No air and no hose; a gland bought and not fitted; a pot spent on the eighth and no air in the harbor since; **a hand lead's line the woman in the net loft says is right on the twenty-fourth, in about nine words, and no knot in it has been taken out**; the ninth seam's mooring out of service with no end of it entered; the north berth out; the third lay full; the tide gang four men short; the cistern charged on a rota that a man does not walk; the fire party's hose dead for the season; the mole-head light not on and the repair account empty since the middle of February; the harbor authority with no boat of its own; the *Kittiwake* on the hard with no cradle; the hard at Ashtide unanswered since the ninth of November; nineteen winters disputed and unpaid, and going up a coast in a file with a harbor authority's own account attached; the *Sea Wren* at the pile; the eleven copies and the four unreached holders not asked; **the twenty marks of iron and the four hundred and forty pounds and the bell-metal gun-port in one heap behind the shed that may not be sold one mark's worth of without a buyer reading the declaration first**; and **Neave Tarrant, nine winters, out since the twenty-eighth of February, not found, one line in the book, and the yard has not gone and has asked nobody anything.**

### 6. What this batch may not be used for

1. **It may not be used to advance the delayed note.** Eleven logged occasions since the twenty-sixth of October, two of them with no call in them, and neither figure has moved and neither is moved by the figure Calder Marr took himself in a boat on the twenty-third. **A figure taken on purpose in a flat calm is a figure and not a note heard out of the weather, and no occasion is entered in this batch, and a man with a slate has four pages in a market and no record of anything said to him in a boat, because a man in a boat is not a man in a market and this yard keeps the two apart.** The answer inside the note is reserved to the Volume 15 midpoint.
2. **It may not be used to spend the naming of the condition.** It is not named in this batch, twice, in the bare form Volume 02 left it in, with a reason against it and nobody asking. **The one naming is therefore still available and is owed to Movement 4, and no Movement 2 or 3 chapter may take it, and no chapter in this batch may be read as having taken it.**
3. **It may not be used to put a fifth name on the writ, or to widen the writ's ground, or to treat the writ as a charter or a renewal, or to manufacture a second season.** The yard refused a man a day in a form he can check **because** four names is a leash and a crew at the same time.
4. **It may not be used to make a return out of a day with nothing in it, to produce a third return, or to widen the reporting clause.** The yard said out loud on a mole on the twenty-second that it has made two and that if a third is asked for it will say it has made two.
5. **It may not be used to lift, narrow, re-serve or alter the notice of the thirty-first, to argue clause two in a shed, to reach the plate at the fifth seam, or to put a ninth acknowledgement in the ledger.** **Both this batch's acknowledgements are spent and Movement 2 enters none.**
6. **It may not be used to name the Nine Harbors, the Quietening or the Crown Seal, to explain the nine-line mark, to explain or name the coast road, to connect it to anything under the town, to name the compartment or to state that the sealed case was in standing space number four, to quote a custody number's digits, to put a senior examiner in a room, to name an office of returning memory, to have Captain Holt appear in a room or at a mole, or to introduce a new antagonist, institution or final enemy.**
7. **It may not be used to open the lead plug's number, to ask the bellwright his one question, to revive the phrase *a tongue half worn through*, to make the bell an instrument, to buy a second going out, to pay a second eight marks, to enter a second going out as done, or to say the going out is anything other than the harbor's bellwright on the harbor's ground on the harbor's account.**
8. **It may not be used to name a cause for the glass storm, to let it speak, to make the man of fifty-one with a bad wheel be interviewed or be put on a lead, to tell the concealment a second time, to ask Sera Mott, to pay Mara Venn, to pay the eight shillings and tenpence, to reduce the forty marks twice, to sell one mark's worth of the heap behind the shed, to weigh, price or offer the bell-metal gun-port, or to treat a man with a bad wheel in a boat as the man of fifty-one of the *Grey Widow*.**
9. **It may not be used to bring back a custody object, to re-write the chandler's note, to move any of its three counts of weeks, to move the count of eleven copies, or to send a document out of a city and get an answer back.** The circuit office's fourth request of the sixteenth of March is unanswered and unrefused and is a different paper.
10. **It may not be used to put a diver in the water who is not a hired man with his own limit fixed at the boat, under a named tender who is not a diver, with a cutoff the tender's to pull, with a reason in the office's book before anybody is wet, in Calder Marr's own left hand with the clerk present and unedited.** One day of this batch had a person in the water — the eighth of May — and the conditions are all on the page.

### 7. The costs this batch closes with

1. **A return two days late, about the yard's own bucket.** And in its keeper's line, unasked, the disclosure that the object was in a shed for six days inside the second clause of a notice this yard has not argued and will not.
2. **A compressor's gland bought on credit and no delivery hose bought at all**, and nine days without air, and the only pot in the harbor spent on a Tuesday.
3. **Two days of the yard's own pump, the first hire out of that shed since the fifth of April, and a day lost for every day, and the yard has declared that it may not have the pump back in June.**
4. **A boy off the stone on Monday the fifteenth of May**, at the rate, for the week, with no speech and no thanks, and **a third column with a head on it and nothing under it and a line under the head saying so.**
5. **A wage week with no line in it on Tuesday the sixteenth of May, and another on the twenty-third**, and a column standing at five lines and twenty marks with the fifth of March in it.
6. **A day of work refused to a man who had better gear than this yard and no instrument**, and a copy given away for nothing with his name on it.
7. **A correct refusal of a named plan, and a claim window spent on a purpose that is not this yard's, and nineteen winters of a herring fleet's dues going up a coast in a file that will make them harder to dispute.**
8. **A boat's day at eight marks, all of it the pump's money, and a man of fifty in it who was owed eight marks and a day of a boat in April and is owed it again in May and is not paid and is not asked to be.**
9. **A sale refused to the first customer the coast road has ever had, and a record of the refusal in his own hand in a market that is not the yard's to correct.**

### 8. Proposed canon changes

**None applied. One flagged rather than applied, and one discipline rather than a rule.**

1. **FLAGGED, NOT APPLIED — the bucket was in a shed for six days, and the yard has disclosed it to a circuit office in its own return.** Chapters 91–100 are silent about where the bucket was after the twenty-eighth of April, and Chapter 100's read-out does not mention it. **Chapter 101 places it in a boat in a shed from the twenty-eighth of April to the fourth of May, which is a continuation of Volume 02's own sentence — the second clause keeps recovered transparent mineral out of every shed and the yard put the bucket in a boat and did not look at it — and it is a self-reported breach of clause two that no earlier chapter entered.** It is on the page, it is in Return 1 unasked, and the harbor authority's clerk has the other half of the figure in his own book. **It is not a canon change to the notice, which is not lifted, is not argued and is not narrowed; it is an addition to the batch's own standing exposure, and a later phase may not soften it, may not have it resolved by an argument, and may not have the yard lifted the notice as a consequence.** A Volume 03 audit may rule on it.
2. **A discipline, not a canon change: the harbor authority has a compressor, a pot and about four fathoms of hose of its own, and the yard has none of the three.** This is on the page twice — the air of the eighth of May and the pot bought on the seventh of April and filled on the twenty-eighth — and it means **a yard with a writ can work a day on the authority's ground in this harbor, and a yard without air can buy air, and what it cannot buy in this harbor is a gland's hose, because there is no hose in this harbor longer than about nine fathoms.** It does not change any institution and it may be used by any later chapter without further ruling.
3. **The eighth of the reeve's acknowledgements is entered on his own motion and is not asked for by anybody, and its price is a claim window and a file.** This is on the page and it is the volume's mechanism for the fifth seam working in the direction lock L7 asks for — **a change of politics in an office, and not a better letter from a shed** — and it is the shape the plate's mechanism takes for the rest of the volume. It is not an amendment of the notice and it reaches nothing.
4. **The counting rule and the two returns are on the page in Calder Marr's own mouth and in the harbor authority's clerk's own mouth** — the day of the finding is not counted, the fourth day after it is the day it falls due, a return entered late is a return entered late and is not a different instrument and is not a smaller one — **and the counting rule is now spoken, not only observed.**
5. **Two things this batch does not do that a later phase must not assume it did: it does not name the condition, and it does not ask the bellwright.** Both are on the page in the negative and both are owed forward.
